## 14. Map implementation

| Topic | Plan |
|---|---|
| Library | MapLibre GL JS, loaded only when the map is shown |
| Basemap | Protomaps basemap (OpenStreetMap data) as a single PMTiles file on our server, served by Caddy with HTTP range requests; glyphs (SIL OFL) and sprites (MIT) self-hosted from `protomaps/basemaps-assets`. The z0–15 planet was 138 GB on 2026-09-30 and the maxzoom-10 extract of that build measured 3.8 GB (one contiguous download); MapLibre over-zooms to street level for context. As built in M5.1: `deploy/basemap/fetch-basemap.sh` puts it in `/opt/gliagora/basemap/public` in versioned folders, and `/basemap/current.json` (never cached) names the current tiles, fonts and sprites, so a refresh needs no deploy and can be rolled back; the tests use a 2.3 MB Istanbul fixture of the same layout |
| Why not OSM's tiles | The OSMF tile servers are donation-funded, best effort, may block heavy users without notice, and warn commercial or donation-seeking services that access can be withdrawn `[V]` |
| Style | Minimal custom style from the Protomaps flavours, tokenised for light (paper) and dark (night); English labels with fallback; localized labels later |
| Data | `/api/map/institutions?{filters}` returns GeoJSON (institution ID, name, city, country, counts of positions and events) using the shared `FilterState`; cached 10 minutes per filter combination. As built in M5.2: the pins carry only the ID and the count; the panel's text (each position with its deadline in words) comes in the same answer, keyed by institution, and the list and the map resolve the filters with the same code (`src/lib/records.ts`), so they always count the same positions |
| Clustering | GeoJSON source with clustering (radius ~50 px, stops at zoom 12), summed counts; clicking a cluster zooms to its expansion zoom |
| Pins | Circle with count; ring style shows positions, events or both; the panel repeats this in text (never colour alone) |
| Zoom story | World → country (click a country in the list or map to fit its bounds) → institution → panel with its positions and events → detail page |
| Panel | Side panel (desktop) or bottom sheet (mobile); focus moves to its heading; `Esc` closes |
| Sync | Same filters as the list; choosing an institution adds an "Institution" chip; hovering a list row highlights its pin on desktop. As built in M5.3: `/en/jobs/?view=map` ("Show the map"), the list on the left and the map on the right (on phones the map first); every filter, chip, page and "Clear all" keeps the view, and the map takes the new pins without being made again; the panel's "These positions in the list" adds the institution's chip and keeps the map |
| Accessibility | Map region labelled with a "Skip map" link; never a keyboard trap; "Institutions in view" list as the keyboard and screen-reader alternative; no geolocation prompt; reduced motion jumps instead of flying |
| Attribution | "© OpenStreetMap contributors" (ODbL, links to the copyright page), Protomaps and MapLibre in the attribution control; ROR credited on the map page; the ODbL notice for deadline time zones derived from OpenStreetMap borders (§11.3) is settled here too. As built in M5: the credits in the map's corner and under the map (with ROR); the time zones credited in the terms' "Material from others" (draft) and on the About page ("Data and credits"), and lawyer question B20 asks whether job pages need their own notice; until it is answered they carry none (D45) |
| Events | Pinned at the host institution or at the venue's city; never at an individual's address |
| Fallback | No WebGL → list + short message (M5.2: no static preview image; a picture made earlier would not show the current pins, while the list next to it does; approved, D45); no JavaScript → the same list, each institution linked to its page |
| Updates | Basemap refreshed twice a year; institution data monthly with the ROR import |

## 15. Legal and compliance

This section is written without legal advice, as requested. Items that cannot be made low-risk by design are flagged with a safer alternative or a deferral (§15.2).

### 15.1 Checklist mapped to features

| Area | Rule | Implementation | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Whole site | Law 5651 Art. 3 and the 2007 regulation: identifying information reachable directly from the home page under a "contact" heading (the regulation names commercial or economic content providers) | "Contact and legal notice" page linked in the footer of every page including home: `hello@gliagora.com` at launch; full name (or company name) and postal address added before any revenue, when the site becomes commercial | `[V]` rule (secondary source for the regulation text); `[U]` our reading that a non-commercial site is outside the regulation's list |
| Links | Law 5651 Art. 4(2): a content provider is not responsible for linked third-party content unless it clearly adopts it | Neutral link text; summaries in our words; no endorsement of employers | `[V]` |
| Removal requests | Law 5651 Art. 9: people whose personal rights are affected can ask the content provider to remove content | Takedown form; acknowledgement within 24 hours `[U]` exact current deadline | `[V]` right; `[U]` deadline |
| Jobs | Law 4904: job placement intermediation, including over the internet, needs İŞKUR permission; publishing ads of unlicensed intermediaries is prohibited and fined | Publisher model; employer-origin listings only; no CVs, applications, matching or fees; Terms and About say so in English and Turkish | `[V]` law; `[U]` no İŞKUR guidance found on facts-only aggregators |
| Aggregation | EU database right (Directive 96/9/EC Art. 7; CJEU C-762/19) | Boards used for discovery only; employer links; source register with terms and robots checks | `[V]` |
| All content | Copyright | Own words; no copied descriptions or abstracts; open-licence images with attribution; no logos | — |
| News | EU press publishers' right excludes very short extracts, but headline reuse is contested `[U]` | Write our own headlines | Safer by design |
| Newsletter and forms | KVKK Art. 10 (information notice), Art. 5 (legal bases); explicit consent kept separate from the notice | Turkish aydınlatma metni + separate açık rıza metni; unticked checkbox; double opt-in; consent log | — |
| Transfers abroad | KVKK Art. 9 as amended in 2024 | Turkey-only processors, with two recorded exceptions: Cloudflare Email Routing (interim until M2, while the site is private, so no published text describes it, D32) and the Claude API (redacted text only); any new service is checked first | `[V]` |
| Processors | KVKK Art. 12 (security, processor contracts) | Written processor agreements with VPS, relay, mail host and backup provider; if someone other than the controller runs the technical side, a short controller–processor agreement between them | — |
| Registry | VERBİS | Natural-person controllers under the Board's thresholds are exempt `[U]` current thresholds; recheck yearly | `[U]` |
| Data subject rights | KVKK Arts. 11 and 13 (answer free of charge within 30 days); GDPR Arts. 12–22 (one month) | Form + `privacy@`; tracker with due dates | Well established; wording `[U]` |
| Breaches | KVKK: notify the Board within 72 hours (Board decision) `[U]`; GDPR Art. 33 (72 hours) | Breach runbook with contacts and templates | `[U]` |
| EU visitors | GDPR applies to offering services to people in the EU; a non-EU controller needs an EU representative (Art. 27) unless processing is occasional | See §15.2 flag 1 | Pricing `[V]`; assessment `[U]` |
| Cookies | ePrivacy Art. 5(3) | Only strictly necessary storage (member and admin session cookies; a theme choice in `localStorage` only if a theme switch is ever added: dark mode follows the system, §7.2); cookieless analytics; no banner | — |
| Email | Double opt-in; one-click unsubscribe (RFC 8058) | listmonk configuration + headers | — |
| Accounts | Member data; provider terms (Google OAuth, ORCID Public API) | Server-side redirect sign-in with minimal scopes; providers named in the privacy policy; no personal data sent to providers (data only flows in) | `[U]` KVKK view of social sign-in (own assessment); ORCID Public API free for non-commercial use `[V]` |
| Maps | OSMF tile policy; ODbL attribution | Self-hosted tiles; visible attribution | `[V]` |
| Structured data | Google content policies | No JobPosting; Event only when eligible (§16) | `[V]` |
| AI assistance | EU AI Act transparency duties for AI-generated public-interest text, with an exception for human-reviewed text under editorial responsibility, from 2 Aug 2026 `[U]` | Human approval required; AI-use statement in the sources policy, which also says that a hub introduction may start from an AI draft that the editor rewrites and approves (D53) | `[U]` |
| Phase 3 user content | EU DSA: hosting services need points of contact, terms, notice and action, statements of reasons; providers without an EU establishment must appoint an EU legal representative (no size exemption); micro and small enterprises are exempt from some reporting and platform duties | DSA readiness is part of the Phase 3 gate | `[V]` (national regulator and secondary sources) |
| Phase 3 user content | Law 5651: hosting providers keep traffic data for 1–2 years `[U]` | Log retention designed before launch of user content | `[U]` |
| Phase 4 sponsorship | Law 6563 and its regulation: commercial electronic messages need prior consent; İYS | Register before the first sponsored issue | `[V]` scope; `[U]` İYS details |

### 15.2 Flags: not fully low-risk by design

| # | Issue | Why | Safer alternative or deferral |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | EU GDPR representative | A monthly newsletter open to EU residents is regular processing, so the "occasional" exemption is unlikely to apply | **Decided 2026-09-26 (D5, updated):** no EU representative; accepted risk at the expected scale (~1,000 members). Revisit only at the first revenue, the Phase 3 gate or any contact from an EU authority (~€420/year upward `[V]`). Main safeguard: instant self-service unsubscribe, export and deletion (§3.2) |
| 2 | İŞKUR classification of job aggregation | No official guidance found | Strict publisher guardrails (§6.1, §15.1); if İŞKUR raises it, pause the jobs window and seek advice |
| 3 | Publishing a home address (Law 5651) | Personal safety and privacy | **Decided (D9):** no address at launch (non-commercial reading `[U]`); before the first revenue, the business address from the tax registration or a virtual office `[U]` |
| 4 | Phase 3 user-generated content | EU representative under the DSA, moderation duties, Turkish hosting-provider log duties | Defer to the Phase 3 gate; start with an institution-level directory from ORCID data and no free-text posts |
| 5 | Phase 4 live talks platform | A hosted video platform account processes attendee data abroad | Public livestream (viewers use the platform directly) + registrations and reminders kept on Gliagora; or self-hosted video conferencing in Turkey `[U]` capacity |
| 6 | AI assistance | Anything sent to the API leaves Türkiye | **Decided 2026-09-26:** listings + papers with redaction, an audit copy of what was sent, and operator approval of every item; residual risk if redaction misses a name. The weekly drafts by Claude Code on the operator's computer (D52) carry the same risk: the redacted text reaches Anthropic in the same way |
| 7 | Learn and Bench to Bedside | Medical misinformation risk | Research-translation scope only (D19); evidence labels, references and trial IDs, not-medical-advice note, links to official patient information; clinician review optional and shown only when done |

### 15.3 Revenue, tax and commercial triggers

Before **any** money arrives (sponsorship, ads, donations, paid events): see an accountant and register for tax **first**. The social-content-producer exemption (GVK 20/B) is unlikely to cover a website: the implementing communiqué excludes personal websites, although the scope was widened in 2024, so this needs an accountant's view `[U]`. Also before the first sponsored email: İYS registration; clear "Sponsored" labels on site and in email; update Terms and Privacy.

**Before-any-revenue checklist** (sponsorship, ads, donations, paid events or services):

- See an accountant and register for tax (see above).
- Add the operator's full name (or company name) and postal address to the contact and legal notice page (D9).
- Run the trademark name searches in TÜRKPATENT, EUIPO and WIPO, and decide whether to register the name (D13).
- İYS registration before the first sponsored email; "Sponsored" labels on the site and in email.
- Update the terms of use and the privacy policy; the operator approves the new texts (D28).

### 15.4 Required legal pages and their contents

The texts are Markdown files in `content/{locale}/` (D28). Each stays a draft (visible notice, `noindex`, not in the sitemap) until the operator approves that exact text; open questions are listed in `docs/legal-open-questions.md`.

| Page | Contents |
|---|---|
| Contact and legal notice (`/en/contact/`, `/tr/iletisim/`) | Launch: email `hello@gliagora.com`; what Gliagora is; statement that Gliagora publishes information and is not an employment agency; links to takedown and data requests. Before any revenue: operator's full name (or company name) and postal address |
| Privacy policy (EN) | Controller: Nuray Sancar, `hello@gliagora.com` (identity required by KVKK Art. 10 and GDPR Art. 13; no postal address needed); data processed (member accounts: email, optional display name, ORCID iD, saved items, preferences; sign-in providers Google and ORCID; newsletter email and consent records; suggestion form; data request and takedown correspondence; server logs with masked IPs, 14 days; cookieless analytics); purposes and legal bases (GDPR and KVKK); recipients (named Turkish processors); no transfers abroad; retention table; rights and how to use them; complaints (KVKK Board, EU supervisory authorities); EU representative; minimum age 16; security summary; version history |
| KVKK aydınlatma metni (TR) | Article 10 elements: controller identity (Nuray Sancar), purposes, recipients and reasons, collection method and legal basis, Article 11 rights, how to apply |
| Açık rıza metni (TR) | Separate, specific consent text for the newsletter |
| Cookies and storage | The few strictly necessary items, why, how long; no analytics cookies |
| Terms of use | Service description; information only, no guarantee; always check the original posting; no employment intermediation; acceptable use of forms; licence of our summaries (quote with attribution and link); third-party links; liability limits as permitted; governing law Türkiye without removing mandatory consumer rights elsewhere `[U]`; changes |
| Notice and takedown, corrections | How to report; what to include; timelines (acknowledgement within 24 hours for personal-rights requests `[U]`, decision usually within 72 hours); employer update/removal requests; counter-notice; corrections log |
| Data requests | Self-service for members (export and delete in settings); verification-link flow with automatic deletion for non-members; form with instant acknowledgement for other requests; free; answer within 30 days. As built in M4b (D49): one form for every kind; its instant email is the acknowledgement and asks the person to confirm the request with a link, and only confirmed requests are acted on (the 30 days count from receipt; §3.2) |
| Accessibility statement | WCAG 2.1 AA target; known issues; how we test; contact; response time |
| Sources and editorial policy | Facts + own summary + link; source categories; automation policy; AI use; corrections; no paid placements; how employers request changes; sponsorship labels (Phase 4) |
| Medical note (Learn) | Not medical advice; evidence levels explained; author credentials and reviewer; last reviewed date; what to do in an emergency |

## 16. SEO and growth

### 16.1 Technical SEO

- Server-rendered HTML for every page; clean, stable URLs; canonical tags; split XML sitemaps with accurate `lastmod`; `robots.txt` disallows `/admin`, `/api`, search and filter URLs. In the M2 state (D41, D50) it allows only the public pages that are indexed, and the sitemaps list only those (§2.2).
- Lifecycle rules from §2.5 (closed jobs `noindex` → `410`); empty institution pages `noindex`.
- Filtered URLs canonicalise to the base list. Indexed landing pages are curated: subfield hubs, and "position type × subfield" pages only when they hold ≥10 open items (avoids thin or mass-generated pages).
- Open Graph images generated on the server from our own fonts and tokens.
- Search Console and Bing Webmaster Tools (DNS verification; no visitor data involved).

### 16.2 Structured data (only where eligible)

| Type | Where | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| `Organization`, `WebSite` | Home, About | Yes |
| `BreadcrumbList` | All deep pages | Yes |
| `Article` (with author) | Learn, Papers of the month, newsletter archive | Yes |
| `ProfilePage` | Author pages | Yes |
| `Event` | Events with a physical venue and registration open to the public | Yes, only then. Google requires a unique page per event, a physical location and public bookability; online-only and invitation or selection-based events are ineligible; the event experience is limited to a few regions and languages `[V]` |
| `JobPosting` | — | **Never.** Google does not allow job postings with incomplete descriptions, and the markup must sit on the page that carries the full description `[V]`; our facts-only pages do not qualify |
| FAQ, Dataset, Course | — | Not used |

### 16.3 Content and trust

- Subfield hubs with an introduction of 150–300 words that the editor writes and approves, live items from all windows and links to related hubs. She may start from an AI draft kept in a field of its own, which only editors see and no public page reads (D53); the hub shows only her introduction, and she holds editorial responsibility for it, as for listings. A hub is indexed only once its English introduction has at least 150 words (D40); a shorter one is shown, but the hub stays `noindex`.
- Author pages with credentials; editorial policy; references and "last reviewed" dates on Learn.
- Internal links from every item to its subfield hubs, institution page and related items.

### 16.4 Growth loops

| Channel | Tactic |
|---|---|
| Newsletter | The core loop: signup blocks on home and every window; archive pages rank and convert |
| Shareable filters | "PhD positions in computational neuroscience in Germany" as a clean URL with a good preview card |
| LinkedIn (positions) | Weekly post of 5–10 closing-soon positions linking to Gliagora pages |
| Instagram @gliagora (later) | Carousels generated from published items: Papers of the month, Deadlines this month, Bench to Bedside explainers; an admin "Export for social" action renders 1080×1350 cards + caption drafts |
| Communities | Society student chapters, university career services, Neuromatch and similar communities, relevant mailing lists (announce the digest) |
| Measurement | Umami (cookieless) with UTM tags on our own links; Search Console queries; newsletter signups per 100 unique visitors (target 2–4%) |

## 17. Accessibility and performance plan

### 17.1 Targets

| Area | Target |
|---|---|
| Accessibility | WCAG 2.1 AA on every public template and the admin views used daily; visible focus everywhere; reflow at 320px; 200% text zoom; forced-colours mode; `prefers-reduced-motion` respected |
| Core Web Vitals (p75, mobile, real users) | LCP ≤2.5 s, INP ≤200 ms, CLS ≤0.1 |
| Server response | TTFB ≤0.6 s for Türkiye and Europe, ≤1.0 s elsewhere (cached HTML) |
| Lab scores (staging, mobile throttling) | Lighthouse performance ≥90 and accessibility 100 on home, jobs, job detail, events, funding, papers |

### 17.2 How it is tested

| Layer | Method | When |
|---|---|---|
| Static checks | `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`; lint rule against hard-coded strings and raw colour values | Every commit |
| Automated a11y | Playwright + axe-core on ~12 templates × light/dark × desktop/mobile; 0 serious or critical issues allowed | Every pull request |
| Keyboard paths | Playwright scripts: skip link, mega-menu, search dialog, filters, bottom sheet, map panel, newsletter form (focus order and focus return); from M4b also the Save toggle (focus stays), the account sub-navigation, the privacy page, the deletion's confirmation page and the outcome messages that take focus after a redirect | Every pull request |
| Contrast | `src/app/(frontend)/tokens.test.ts` validates all token pairs in §7.2 | Every pull request |
| Performance lab | Lighthouse with the budgets of §17.3 and the lab targets of §17.1, against the deployed site, on the main templates, as a phone and as a desktop: `pnpm lighthouse:prod` (`tools/lighthouse`, built 2026-10-07). Run from the operator's computer, not in CI: a run takes about 15 minutes, and CI minutes are limited (D46). The site-wide password reaches it only piped on standard input, and no report holds it (D42). The list templates, empty on the server before the launch, are measured locally with the fake data too (`pnpm lighthouse:local`) | Before the launch (pre-launch checklist, §20) and after a change that could move the numbers. Byte budgets and font layout shift are checked on every pull request already |
| Manual a11y | Keyboard-only walkthrough; screen readers: Orca (Linux), VoiceOver (iOS/macOS) or TalkBack (Android); zoom and forced-colours checks | On the deployed site, from S on; part of the pre-launch checklist (§20) |
| Real device | Mid-range Android phone on a throttled 4G connection | On the deployed site, from S on; part of the pre-launch checklist (§20) |
| Real-user monitoring | `web-vitals` in the browser → aggregated events in Umami (no personal data) → p75 per template and region | Continuous; weekly review |

### 17.3 Budgets

| Resource | Budget |
|---|---|
| HTML (list pages) | ≤60 KB gzip |
| CSS (total) | ≤35 KB gzip |
| JavaScript on ordinary pages (initial, incl. framework runtime) | ≤140 KB gzip (D29). Measured at M1: 135 KB on every template, of which about 132 KB is React and Next.js themselves; the header's own script is about 3 KB. After the savings pass of D45 (2026-10-01): 134.58 KB on every page and 136.33 KB on the jobs list (was 139.90), which now has a budget of its own, ≤137 KB (`budgets.spec.ts`), so the saving stays. Of the 134.58 KB, 131.84 KB are React, Next.js 16.3 and Turbopack's runtime (chunks that hold nothing else; a webpack build was no smaller) and 2.74 KB the header's script; the jobs list adds 1.75 KB of its own (0.7 KB of it its error state). The ≤130 KB D45 asked for the jobs list is therefore below what the framework alone needs; the operator decided after M6 (D47): ordinary pages keep ≤140 KB, and the jobs list keeps its own cap of ≤137 KB in place of the 130 KB target. The Save button's stub in the header's script (M4b, D49; the rest loads on first use) measured 0.12 KB on every page (134.58 → 134.70 KB) and 0.13 KB on the jobs list (136.48 → 136.61 KB with #67, signed out and as a member alike; 136.60 KB from #68 on), so the jobs list keeps its cap. The account page with its spam-checked form for adding an address (M4b): 136.96 KB, the same as the sign-in page's form; the privacy page and the confirmation page of an account deletion also 136.96 KB (the sign-in panel they show to a visitor who is signed out brings the same script), the page after a deletion 134.70 KB; the jobs list with a member's preferred subfields applied (D35) and with `prefs=off`: 136.60 KB. The data-request page with its form (M4b, #70): 137.30 KB, about as much as the suggest and report forms (137.37 and 137.24 KB). The M2 state's shell (D50; the header's links read the address a visitor sees, and Ctrl+K checks for the search dialog) adds about 0.13 KB to every page: ordinary pages 134.83 KB, the jobs list 136.74 KB (0.26 KB below its cap), the data-request page 137.44 KB. The map and hero chunks below have their own budgets and load only on their own routes |
| Hero chunk (home only, after `load`) | ≤230 KB gzip |
| Map chunk (on demand) | ≤330 KB gzip (approved after M5, D45; measured: about 323 KB, of which MapLibre GL JS 6 with its worker is 308 KB; the earlier unverified estimate was 280 KB), plus MapLibre's stylesheet (about 10 KB); loaded only where a map is shown, after the page |
| Fonts | 2 families; ≤4 files preloaded; ≤120 KB total |
| Images | AVIF/WebP, responsive `srcset`, explicit dimensions, lazy below the fold |
| Third-party requests | 0 |

The budgets above for JavaScript, CSS, HTML and fonts (bytes, preloaded files and families) are checked for every template in CI by `tests/e2e/budgets.spec.ts`, measured from what the browser downloads, and the end-to-end tests fail on any request to another domain. The lab scores and the Core Web Vitals in the lab (§17.1) are measured with Lighthouse by `pnpm lighthouse:prod` against the deployed site and by `pnpm lighthouse:local` against a local build (§17.2; `tools/lighthouse`, a project of its own, so that Lighthouse reaches neither the app's dependencies nor CI), which count the same budgets the same way. Measured locally on 2026-10-07 with the fake data, 17 templates: as a desktop (Lighthouse's estimate), performance 100 and accessibility 100 on every template, LCP 0.5 to 0.6 s; as a phone with the browser slowed down for real (slow 4G, a processor four times slower), performance 97 to 100, accessibility 100, LCP 1.5 to 1.6 s, CLS 0 and TBT at most 84 ms. Lighthouse's own estimate of a phone, made from a fast load, gave LCP 1.8 to 2.6 s on the same build (2.6 s on the jobs list and on the jobs list with its map): on one computer every file arrives before the first paint, and the estimate then counts all of them into LCP, so it is the time until the whole page has loaded. Measured against the deployed site on 2026-10-16 (behind the site-wide password, with a temporary second user for the run; 16 templates, no job page while the server has no positions): every target and budget met. As a phone (Lighthouse's estimate), performance 98 to 100, accessibility 100, LCP 1.4 to 2.3 s, CLS 0, TBT at most 80 ms, the server's answer 16 to 202 ms; as a desktop, performance and accessibility 100 everywhere, LCP 0.3 to 0.7 s.

### 17.4 Techniques

Server Components by default (lists and filters render on the server; only controls are client components); static generation with on-demand revalidation when content is published; optimistic filter chips and `useTransition` to keep INP low while results load; metric-adjusted font fallbacks and reserved aspect ratios for zero layout shift; link prefetch on hover/focus for detail pages; map and hero code only where needed.

## 18. Security and privacy by design

### 18.1 Personal data inventory at launch (the only personal data processed)

| Data | Where | Purpose | Retention |
|---|---|---|---|
| Newsletter email + subscription status | listmonk DB (Türkiye), confirmed subscribers only; a signup waiting for its confirmation is an email link in the app DB (left out of backups) | Send the digest | Until unsubscribe (deleted at once then, by the link or one click; when a member stops it in the settings, within minutes, usually at once; also when a member deletes the account on purpose or confirms a deletion request; an account deleted for inactivity leaves it); unconfirmed: 7 days, in the app DB only (D50) |
| Member account (email, optional display name, ORCID iD, provider account IDs) | App DB (Türkiye) | Sign-in, saved items, preferences | Until deletion; after 24 months inactive: warning, deletion 30 days later; accounts without an email address (nobody to warn): deletion 24 months and 30 days after the last activity, which the account page tells them (D49) |
| Saved items, saved searches, preferences | App DB | Member features | Deleted with the account |
| Sessions and email links (sign-in, adding an address, confirming a data request, confirming a Glia Notes signup) | App DB, left out of backups | Keep members signed in; prove an address | Sessions 30 days from sign-in; sign-in links 15 minutes, address and data-request links 24 hours (with the address and, for a data request, what the form said), Glia Notes links 7 days (with the address, its language and the consent text's version); single use; only SHA-256 of each token stored |
| Sign-in cookie (sealed: the state of a Google or ORCID sign-in, the new account waiting for "Create account", an email link's browser binding) | Visitor's browser | Complete a sign-in safely | 15 minutes (D49) |
| Newsletter cookie (`__Host-gliagora-newsletter`, sealed: the address the confirmation link went to and when, and the time and consent-text version of the form) | Visitor's browser | "Check your inbox" and "Send it again" | 1 hour from the form, never extended (D50) |
| Consent records (listmonk's subscriber UUID and ID, event, text version, time, form; no address) | App DB, carried over a restore | Prove consent | 3 years after the subscription ended (its unsubscribe through the site, or the day the site noticed that listmonk no longer has the subscriber, checked every 10 minutes), or after their own time once unlinked `[U]`; the records of the subscriptions an erasure ends (an account deleted on purpose, a confirmed deletion request) are unlinked: listmonk's UUID and ID removed, which is not anonymisation (the request's record, kept 2 years with the address, and the deletion log share the erasure's time; a member's row keeps the time of the last choice); records of subscriptions that had ended before are not (D50) |
| Suggestion form email (optional) | App DB | Tell the submitter the outcome | 90 days after decision |
| Hashed IP addresses for the rate limits of both public forms (suggest a listing, report a problem); from M4b also of the sign-in, address and data-request forms, hashed email addresses for the limits of the forms that send an email, and a count per member (by account ID) for the form that adds or changes an account's address; from M2 also of the Glia Notes signup ("Send it again" counts with it), with a minute between two of its emails per hashed address | App DB only (`form-limits`, left out of backups) | Protect the forms from abuse, and inboxes from repeated emails | 24 hours (D34, D49, D50) |
| Data request / takedown correspondence | App DB + mailbox | Handle the request | 2 years after closure `[U]`; account deletions in the settings are recorded as data requests too (D49) |
| Unconfirmed data requests: kind, date and a keyed hash of the address (no address, name or details) | App DB | Show that a request arrived | 60 days (D49) |
| Deletion log: record IDs of deletions a person asked for or the inactivity rule made (from M2 also listmonk's subscriber IDs of those who unsubscribed, stopped Glia Notes in their account or whose data was erased, and the IDs of the consent records an erasure unlinked, D50), with the time, no personal data | App DB | Repeat those deletions if a backup is restored (only on records created no later than the deletion, §18.2) | 7 months, longer than any backup holds the deleted data (D49) |
| Server logs | VPS | Security and debugging | 14 days: Caddy's access and error logs (IP masked), and the system journal, which holds the containers' logs (the app's, Caddy's own, listmonk's) and the server's own, whose SSH and fail2ban lines keep full IP addresses (D34, D50). Open point: the firewall's blocked packets (`/var/log/ufw.log`, `kern.log`) and fail2ban's own log (`/var/log/fail2ban.log`), with full addresses, are kept about four weeks by rsyslog and logrotate (lawyer review B11) |
| Analytics | Umami DB | Aggregated statistics (no cookies) | Aggregates kept; raw events 12 months |
| Admin accounts | App DB | Access control | While active |

### 18.2 Controls

| Area | Measures |
|---|---|
| Admin access | Payload auth with lockout (5 attempts, 15 minutes); long passphrase in a password manager; second factor (TOTP plugin if mature `[U]`, otherwise Caddy basic auth as a second gate on `/admin` and `/api`, which covers Payload's REST routes with its sign-in, password reset and first-account route; GraphQL is off); rate-limited auth routes (Payload's lockout, at most one reset email per account every 15 minutes; Caddy's limit on the sign-in and password routes, 20 a minute per address, below, D42, D51); `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Strict` cookies, accepted on API requests only from the site's own origin (`serverURL`, Payload's CSRF list); audit log. The gate does not cover Payload's admin server functions, which Next.js also answers on public paths: signed out they refuse or render only fixed admin text, and a session needs the gated sign-in, so TOTP stays the real second factor. A public API route (the map, M5) is opened in Caddy one path at a time. listmonk's admin only through an SSH tunnel to the server's own loopback (no route in Caddy, no subdomain), with its own login, whose password is generated on the server (D50); Umami's admin behind basic auth plus its own login |
| Private phase | Until M2 (D32), Caddy asks for the same second password on every path except `/health/` (the uptime check), so nothing is public or indexed before the privacy texts are live. At M2 it opens only what goes public then; jobs and the other sections stay behind it until the launch gate (§3.4). As built (D50): one setting, `SITE_STAGE` (`private` by default, or `m2`), which Compose passes to Caddy and to the app alike. In the M2 state Caddy opens exactly the paths of `deploy/caddy/m2-public.caddy` (each page with and without its slash, `GET`/`HEAD`, `POST` only for the data-request form and the route behind its button, and the Glia Notes form and the routes behind its buttons, among them the one-click unsubscribe of mail programs; Next.js's own files by prefix and each logo of `public/brand/` by name; never a folder, never `/admin`, `/api`, `/basemap` or `/m2/`), written by `pnpm caddy:public` from the app's one list of public pages (`src/lib/public-pages.ts`), in which a legal text appears only once it is approved (`draft: false`), with the pages that belong to it (the Glia Notes signup and its pages with the açık rıza metni, whose consent the signup records); every other path keeps the password. Only in their exact spelling: Caddy's path matcher ignores case and decodes the path, which the app does not, so a regexp on the raw address lets only the listed spelling through (`/EN/` or `/en/%61bout/` keep the password). The app's 404s and 410s reach a visitor only as a plain "Not Found", never as the 404 page with the full navigation. The list on the server is copied by hand and can be ahead of the running image (a rollback past an approval) or behind it, so the two mark each other (`PUBLIC_MARK`, `X-Gliagora-Public`): Caddy marks the requests it opens, and the app refuses a marked request for an address its own image does not hold public with a plain 404 before any page or route runs; the app marks its public answers, and Caddy passes an answer to a page, a machine file or a form only with that mark, so an image from before the M2 state shows nothing either. A value of `SITE_STAGE` that is no stage stops Caddy from starting (`import "stage-{$SITE_STAGE:private}"`, quoted, so `m2` with a space or a comment after it is no stage either, as in the app), so a typing mistake opens nothing. The rewrite to the M2 versions keeps the query (an emailed link's token), which Next.js repeats in `X-Middleware-Rewrite`: Caddy removes that header from the answers and both logs. CI runs the production Caddyfile in front of an M2 app and the app under test (`tests/e2e/m2.spec.ts`) |
| Member accounts | No passwords; members are never Payload accounts (separate module and tables, hidden, `nobody` for every operation and no API routes: `endpoints: false`), so they cannot reach `/admin` or the API. Cookies: `__Host-gliagora-session` (random 256-bit token, only its SHA-256 stored, 30 days from sign-in, never extended) and `__Host-gliagora-signin` (AES-256-GCM sealed, 15 minutes), both `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`, `SameSite=Lax` (the provider's redirect back must carry the sign-in cookie; POSTs are also origin-checked). OAuth: state, nonce (always required) and PKCE where the provider advertises it, ID-token signature checked. Email links single use, consumed only for their own purpose, rate-limited per IP and per hashed address (one budget shared by every form that sends an email, and a site-wide cap); a sign-in link opened in another browser than the one that asked shows a warning. A position to save after signing in (a Save link's) is named on every page on the way (sign-in, "Create account", the emailed link's page), and a start of signing in with Google or ORCID that carries one from anywhere but the sign-in page (`Sec-Fetch-Site`) is sent to that page first, since the provider may answer without a page of its own. Session rotation at sign-in; every change of sign-in methods ends the member's other sessions; two existing accounts are never merged. The site's own origin comes from `SERVER_URL` (or a loopback `Host` locally), never from the request URL (`appOrigin()`); every POST route checks it (`sameOrigin()`: `Sec-Fetch-Site`, else `Origin`) and answers with a relative `303`. Public-form Server Actions refuse calls that Next.js forwarded from another page (`x-action-forwarded`), so at M2 a public page cannot run a form that is still behind the password. Caddy removes the query strings of sign-in, account, data-request and newsletter addresses in both its access log and its error log; Umami's tracker gets `data-exclude-search` (M2). The test providers (`AUTH_TEST_ISSUER`) and ORCID's sandbox are refused where `PAYLOAD_JOBS_AUTORUN` is `true`, and the production files never name the test variable. A restore carries the deletion log, the data-request records and (from M2, D50) the consent log of Glia Notes over and re-applies the logged deletions (`deletion-log`), only to records created no later than the logged deletion, since a restore also sets the ID sequences back; before the app starts, it moves the sequences of `deletion_log`, `data_requests`, `consent_log`, `members`, `submissions` and `reports` past every ID it carried over or that the log names (D49, D50). From M2, `applyDeletionLog` also deletes again, in listmonk, every logged subscriber that a restore of listmonk's database brought back |
| Forms | Server-side validation (zod); ALTCHA; honeypot; rate limits (per form and hashed address, in the app; Caddy's limit in front, below); http/https URL allowlist; length limits; no HTML input; output encoding |
| Rate limits in Caddy | Per visitor address (an IPv4 address, or an IPv6 /64), before the password and the app (`deploy/Caddyfile`, `(rate_limits)`; D42, D51): the editors' sign-in and password routes of Payload's API and the admin's pages for them (not its first-account page), 20 a minute; member sign-in (everything under `/en/sign-in/`, and what the account pages' forms send), 120 a minute; what the public forms send (suggest, report, data request, the Glia Notes signup, "Send it again" and the two confirmations; not the one-click unsubscribe, which mail providers send from a few addresses), 30 a minute; search and filtered lists (`/en/search/`, and the jobs list and the map's data with any query, since Caddy cannot parse every query the app reads), 120 a minute. Counted over 30 or 15 seconds (10, 30, 15 and 30 requests): the limiter keeps a record for every address it sees, also before the password, and short counts keep that record small and soon forgotten; records are cleared every 10 seconds and the `caddy` container has a memory limit (measured: one request from each of the 65,536 networks of one IPv6 /48 to three zones takes Caddy from 60 to about 355 MB for a minute or so). A guard against floods and guessing, far above what people do: ordinary use sends one request per action to these routes, and the app's own limits on the forms (5 to 20 an hour) answer first, with their own messages; people behind one IPv4 address share its counts. A request over a limit gets `429` with `Retry-After` and one line of text, and never reaches the app. Nothing more is kept about visitors: a refused request is one access-log line like any other (masked address), the module's own log line (the whole address) is left out of Caddy's log, its metrics are off, and the counts live in Caddy's memory for less than a minute. With an answer of its own for the `429`, Caddy writes the errors of requests (the reason for a `502`) only at its debug level; the access log keeps each such request. HTTP/3 early data (0-RTT) is off, since its sender's address is not checked yet. No proxy stands in front of Caddy, so the address is the connection's own |
| HTTP headers | Strict CSP with nonces and no third-party origins; HSTS (preload after 3 stable months); `X-Content-Type-Options`; `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`; `Permissions-Policy` disabling camera, microphone, geolocation; `frame-ancestors 'none'` |
| Server | Ubuntu LTS; unattended security upgrades; SSH keys only, no root login; the admin user uses sudo without a password (it logs in only with a key and is in the docker group, which gives root anyway), and the deploy user is not an admin and may run only the deploy script (D42); firewall (80, 443, restricted SSH); fail2ban; non-root containers (listmonk's image runs as root inside its container, with every capability dropped and no new privileges); PostgreSQL not exposed, listmonk only on the server's loopback; one DB user per app; secrets in `600` env files and GitHub Actions secrets |
| Supply chain | Lockfile; Renovate pull requests weekly, merged after staging; dependency vulnerability scan in CI; container images pinned by digest; the server's Caddy image is built in CI from sources fixed by `go.mod` and `go.sum` (`deploy/caddy-image`) |
| Backups | Nightly encrypted restic snapshots to a second location in Türkiye (7 daily, 4 weekly, 6 monthly); automated monthly restore test of each database into an isolated container of its own; quarterly full restore drill; recovery point 24 h, recovery time 4 h. Erased subscribers age out of backups within 6 months (stated in the privacy policy) |
| Monitoring | External uptime check (public URLs only); the server check (`deploy/monitor/check.sh`, every 15 minutes; D51), which reports by email: a file system at least 85% full (space or inodes), less than 10% of the memory available or programs waiting for memory during 10% of the last five minutes, and the site's certificate (also `www.`) with less than a sixth of its lifetime left (15 days of 90; Caddy renews at 30), ended or unreadable. A problem is reported once a second run sees it, then once a day while it lasts; one that comes and goes is one problem (over after an hour without it), a report counts only once its email has gone, and what was seen is kept in memory (`/run`), so that a full disk cannot keep it from being remembered. The emails go through the backup emails' script, so they start with them; backup emails (`deploy/backup/alert.sh`, D42, D50): every failure, and the success of the Sunday backup and of the monthly restore test, so a week without one means trouble; they start once the mailbox's SMTP settings and the recipient (`ALERT_EMAIL`) are set |
| Incidents | Runbook: contain, assess, decide on notification (KVKK Board and, if appointed, EU representative; 72-hour clocks), document; contact list kept in the repo (no personal data) |
| Privacy defaults | No third-party requests; no tracking pixels; masked IPs; cookieless analytics; only the origin sent as referrer; no geolocation prompts; notices at the point of collection |
| Staging hygiene | Fake data only; email allowlist; `noindex`; basic auth |
| AI and tooling hygiene | No personal data in prompts, issues, commits or screenshots; code-only context for Claude Code |
| Vendor control | `docs/vendors.md` lists every external service, the data it sees and its location; adding a vendor requires a KVKK check first |

**Access rules (after M4).** Signed out, Payload's API gives nothing: no collection or global can be read, created, changed or deleted through it, and it grants no permission. Every collection and global states who may do each operation (`src/lib/access.ts`: signed-in editors, or nobody); a rule written as a function replaces Payload's default user check, so it checks the user itself (M4.1 found a delete rule that did not). What is public is drawn by the pages, which read through Payload's local API with their own queries (open jobs only; never drafts, jobs in review, suggestions or reports), and the public forms write through it. `tests/e2e/access.spec.ts` enforces this: it takes every collection and global from `src/payload-types.ts` (which CI checks is up to date), sends signed-out requests for every operation (with real IDs, read by the test editor, of records in every state a rule for single records could treat differently: drafts, jobs in review, withdrawn jobs, suggestions with and without a candidate, reports, an institution added by hand; each must exist in the test data) and fails on any answer that is not a refusal, any granted permission, any email address or any value from the suggestions, reports and unpublished jobs, and on any change to the data; a new collection needs an entry in its table, and a deliberately public read a reason there. `src/lib/access.test.ts` checks the rules as written, field rules included.

## 19. Weekly workload estimate (operator)

| Period | Hours/week | What the hours go to | Fits 15 h? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Build to public launch (M0–M6 incl. M4b, about 11–14 weeks) | 10–14 | Review each milestone on the deployed site (phone; production behind the password while there is no staging, D42), decisions, legal text review (4–6 h); seed ≥150 positions and institutions (6–8 h) | Yes |
| Phase 1a live | 5–8 | Jobs queue (3–4), newsletter (~0.75), inbox, requests and member support (1–1.5), maintenance (0.5–1) | Yes |
| Phase 1 complete | 10–13 | Jobs (3–4), events (1–1.5), funding (0.5–1, quarterly peaks), papers (1.5), news (0.5–1), newsletter (1), Learn and resources (1.5–2), operations and inbox (1–1.5) | Yes, tight in Oct–Feb application season |
| Phase 2 | 12–15 | Above + career guide per month, Turkish review, account support | At the limit; use AI drafts and trim news volume |
| Phase 3 | 15–20 | Above + moderation and member support | No: needs a second editor or moderator (gate) |
| Phase 4 | +6–8 in talk months | Speaker coordination, hosting, recording and summary | Needs a helper |

## 20. Build roadmap

Milestones run in this order: M0 → M1 → M3 → M4 (built and tested locally while S waits, D38) → **S (one-time server setup)** → one-time lawyer review → M2 → M4b → M5 → M6 → M7 … M14 (D23, D32, D38, D39). At the operator's request, M5 and M6's home page were built ahead of the lawyer review and M2 (2026-10-01, D45), and M4b after them (2026-10-01 to 2026-10-02, D48), all behind the site-wide password; then M2's technical parts that need neither the mailbox nor the lawyer (listmonk, the signup with double opt-in, unsubscribe, the consent log, the members' sync, the M2 state of the site prepared but switched off; from 2026-10-02, D50), also behind the password, with no email sent and nothing public; and M11, the newsletter builder, ahead of M8–M10 (from 2026-10-17, D53), behind the password, with pushing an issue to listmonk switched off until the launch, since the launch issue is the first (D41); M6's public launch keeps its place, and accounts stay private until then (D39). The server setup came after M3 so that nothing depends on a live server earlier; M2 (newsletter) follows S and the lawyer review because it needs the mailbox, listmonk and the server, and it is when the first legal texts go live. The whole site stays private until M2 (D32). At M2 only the coming-soon page, the newsletter signup, the About page and the legal texts the operator has approved go public; jobs and the other sections stay private until the M6 public launch, once the §3.4 gate is met (D39, D41).

Until S, each milestone ends merged to `main` with CI green and its image in GHCR. From S on, each milestone ends deployed to production, which doubles as staging while the site is private (D42).

| # | Milestone | Scope | Deployable outcome | Estimate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M0 | Foundations | Repository with `PLAN.md` and `CLAUDE.md`; Next.js + Payload + PostgreSQL in Docker Compose; CI that builds the images and pushes them to GHCR; server files prepared but not run (`deploy/`: setup script, Compose with Caddy, backups, manual deploy workflow) | "Coming soon" page with contact and legal notice, merged to `main` | 1–2 weeks |
| M1 | Design system and shell | Tokens, fonts, header and mega-menu, footer, skip link, dark mode, `/en/` routing, i18n scaffolding, 404/410, legal pages (EN + TR), About with the name story | Shell site with legal pages | 1–2 weeks |
| M3 | Taxonomy and institutions | Taxonomy seed with synonyms; ROR import job; country sets; institution pages; hub skeletons | Admin-complete data layer; hubs `noindex` | 1 week |

Decided during S (D42): no staging while the site is private; automatic rollback on failed health checks; Caddy rate limits on the pre-launch checklist; backup alerts at M2; repository secrets with a restricted deploy user | **Done 2026-09-30** (S notes below): production server running; first deploy through the deploy workflow; the site stays private (D32), and how its private sections stay private once the M2 pages are public is set up at M2 (D39, D41) | 0.5–1 week |

From M4b (D49): the M4b deploy files on the server, with Caddy's log filters for the sign-in, account and data-request addresses, before `SMTP_HOST` is set (`deploy/README.md` step 9), since `SMTP_HOST` also switches on email sign-in (still behind the password); the data-request page goes public with its form, once approved (`/en/legal/data-requests/`, its sub-paths and the form's POST to it); the reduced header leaves out the Account link; Umami's tracker gets `data-exclude-search="true"` (no query string, so no link token, reaches its database); `eraseNewsletter()` (now empty) and the sync of members' digest choices to listmonk, with their `ConsentLog` rows; the site-wide cap on emails sent while people wait (now 50 an hour and 200 a day) set from the mailbox's sending limit; a time limit for container logs (Docker rotates them by size, so on a quiet site they outlive the 14 days the privacy texts give; found during M4b, older than it) | Signups open before content exists: the coming-soon page, the newsletter signup, the About page and the legal texts the operator has approved go public (unapproved drafts stay private), with a reduced header and footer that link only to public pages; until M6, links to the suggest and report forms point to `hello@gliagora.com`; everything else stays private until M6 (D39, D41). The home page, built ahead of M2 (M6, D45), stays private too: the public `/en/` shows the coming-soon text again (M0's, in the git history: `home.comingSoon`, `home.writeToUs`), chosen by the same setting as the reduced header and footer. No Glia Notes issue before M6 (D41). **Built ahead, behind the password (D50; 2026-10-02 to 2026-10-05; #74–#78; M2 notes below):** listmonk (on the server since 2026-10-05), the signup with double opt-in, the welcome email, unsubscribe, the consent log, the members' sync and `eraseNewsletter()`, the M2 state (prepared, switched off), the email cap as a setting, the journal's 14 days and the backup emails. **Still M2's own:** the mailbox with SPF, DKIM, DMARC and the MX move, the `SMTP_*` settings, the lawyer review and the operator's approvals, the switch (`deploy/README.md` section 13), Umami, the archive template (M11), release-tag deploys | 1–2 weeks |

Accounts ready on the deployed site, still private, released with M6 (D39) | 1.5–2 weeks |

| M12 | Resources and Learn | Resources; articles; Bench to Bedside; evidence labels; author pages; hubs for all windows | All six windows live | 2 weeks |

**M1 notes (2026-09-27).**
- Legal pages delivered in M1 as drafts: contact and legal notice (EN, TR), privacy policy, KVKK aydınlatma metni, terms of use. The açık rıza metni (newsletter signup), notice and takedown, data requests, and cookies and storage were drafted after M3 (2026-09-27), before the one-time lawyer review between S and M2, so that one review covers them all (C1, D32); the takedown and data-request pages get their forms in M4 and M4b. The accessibility statement and the sources and editorial policy follow before the M6 launch, as part of the "all legal pages live" gate in §3.4.
- The §12 pseudo-locale screenshot tests are added in M4, when filters and cards exist. In M1 the header is checked in Turkish (labels about 50% longer than English), with WCAG 1.4.12 text spacing and at 320px on every template.
- Lighthouse CI, the screen-reader check and the real-phone check (§17.2) run on the deployed site, so they are on the pre-launch checklist below; byte budgets and layout shift while fonts load are already checked in CI.

**M3 notes (2026-09-27).**
- Taxonomy: the 117 terms of §4.2–4.7 are seeded in English with descriptions and synonyms; Turkish labels come with the Turkish site (§12). Synonyms leave out words that would tag unrelated adverts (bare acronyms such as LTD, MS, PI, BIDS; words such as "award", "virtual", "master's"). The tag suggester (M7) matches all-caps synonyms case-sensitively and as whole words, and prefers the longest match within a vocabulary.
- Geography: 250 countries (UN M49 plus XK and TW) and the four country sets, each checked against its official source on 2026-09-27 (§4.6).
- Institutions: the full ROR v2.13 import took about 15 minutes locally (140,078 institutions); later imports change only what ROR changed. The server checks Zenodo every night at 03:30 UTC; the admin shows the state under System → ROR import. Since M4 (D40) each institution also has a time zone, which the import (and every save) takes from the coordinates and the country when they are new or change, and otherwise leaves as it is (see the M4 notes).
- Institution pages and subfield hubs are built and `noindex`: institution pages become indexable with their first active item (M4), hubs with their written introductions (of at least 150 words, D40). The proxy resolves record URLs (404 for unknown, 308 to the current URL, 410 for a retired subfield, 503 while the database is unreachable) and keeps those answers out of caches.
- Later milestones pick up: the institution mini map (M5), re-checking the country sets (M9), and the MeSH and OpenAlex mappings of the terms (M10).

**M4 notes (2026-09-27; built locally with fake data, D38).**
- Jobs (M4.1): a deadline is a calendar day plus the time zone of the institution, or of the posting when it names another (the 418 zones of the IANA zone.tab and UTC; left empty only when the zone is unknown, which means Anywhere on Earth, as §2.5 and §3.2 say), and `deadlineAt` is when that day ends there. Payload's own time-zone option for date fields is not used: it stores the day as noon in the chosen zone, so the stored date depends on the zone, and choosing the zone after the day shifts the day shown.
- Deadline time zones prefilled (D40, after M4): an institution's `timeZone` comes from its coordinates, offline (`@photostructure/tz-lookup`, §11.3; places at sea give none), checked against its country; ROR gives coordinates, not zones. It is filled when the institution is created, when its coordinates or country change (also through the ROR import) and whenever the field is empty; a zone an editor chose stays until the coordinates or the country change. On the 140,098 locations of the ROR v2.13 dump the lookup alone gave 148 a zone with other UTC offsets than the exact borders (geo-tz, 71 MB, not used), and not only near borders: American Samoa got Samoa's zone (a day ahead), Timor-Leste Indonesia's (an hour behind). So a zone of another country than the institution's (zone.tab) becomes the country's own zone with the same offsets, or its first when all its zones have the same offsets (American Samoa has one), or else stays empty for the editor (a deadline without a zone ends Anywhere on Earth: late, never early). With the check, 140,032 get the zone of the exact borders or one with the same offsets all year, 22 none, and 43 other offsets, nearly all a zone of their own country that the check cannot tell apart (20 places in Xinjiang get Asia/Urumqi where the exact borders give Beijing time); an editor corrects them. The migration fills in the zone of the institutions saved before (about 9 s for the 140,098 of a full ROR import). A job created without a zone (an accepted candidate, the fake data, the API) takes its institution's; an empty zone given on purpose stays empty (Anywhere on Earth), and no save of an existing job changes it, so a published deadline never moves. In the job form, choosing an institution fills the zone when the field is empty or still holds the previous institution's zone, with a note that it was taken from the institution; a zone the editor chose stays, and the editor can change or clear it.
- Review and publication need the facts (position type, at least one subfield, own summary, original posting, deadline, the day it was verified); publication also needs the five checklist boxes (§6.4) and a deadline that has not passed. A job that was published never goes back to draft or review and is never deleted (it is withdrawn instead); only a published job can be expired or withdrawn. Following the §6.3 verification rule, publishing an expired or withdrawn job again, or changing the deadline of a published one, needs a new "Last verified" day (later than the saved one, or today), and closing a published job clears "position still open" and "deadline confirmed", so they are ticked again before it is published again.
- Only signed-in editors delete anything: an `access` function replaces Payload's default user check, so each one checks the user itself (the `/api` routes answer anyone who reaches them; see the access audit after M4). An institution is kept while a job or an earlier version of a job refers to it.
- The duplicate warning (§6.3) runs when a job enters review or publication, and whenever its title, address, institution or deadline change, including when "Duplicate of" is cleared. Withdrawn jobs and jobs already marked as a duplicate are not compared; "Not a duplicate" is not stored and applies to the save it is ticked on; a job marked as a duplicate cannot be published. The canonical address treats http and https as the same and writes percent-escapes in upper case (a comparison key only; the original address is kept as entered).
- A job's `country` and `city` are copied on save from its location override or its institution, for filters and the map; if a ROR update moves an institution, its jobs follow on their next save.
- `source` waits for the Source register (M7). `pnpm fake:jobs` adds about 60 generated jobs and seven fixed ones for the end-to-end tests; it refuses to run on the production server.
- Job pages (M4.2): a job is closed from the end of its deadline day, even before the hourly check marks it expired, and answers `410` from 30 days after it closed (the end of the deadline day, or the moment it was marked expired if that came first, as for a position filled early); a withdrawn job answers `410` at once. The closed page shows the notice and the similar open positions first and no way to apply; it is `noindex`. The deadline shows its time zone ("until the end of that day, Central European Time (UTC+1)") only when the posting's zone is known: Anywhere on Earth is our safe assumption for keeping a listing open, not the employer's deadline, so it is not presented as one. Days left count from the moment, not the reader's calendar; under 24 hours the text says "Last day". Dates are formatted in British English ("14 Nov 2026"; September is "Sept"). On narrow screens at least 20rem (320px) tall the primary action is fixed at the bottom; the page keeps room for it, also when enlarged text wraps its label, and focused elements scroll clear of it (WCAG 2.4.11). On shorter screens (such as 400% zoom) and in print it stays in the page. Titles in another language get `lang`, and right-to-left ones (Arabic, Hebrew, Persian) `dir="rtl"`. A retired subfield stays on its jobs as plain text, without a link to its hub (which answers `410`), and is not used for the breadcrumb. In lists, "View original posting" is an outlined button, so one accent button leads each page; links to employers have no `rel` (editorial, unpaid links; the default referrer policy sends only our origin). Breadcrumbs (Jobs › position type › first subfield › title; on institution pages Home › institution) also give the `BreadcrumbList` data on every page that has breadcrumbs. Redirects from an outdated slug are not cached, for jobs and institutions alike (a title or name changed and changed back would otherwise loop). Institution pages list up to 10 open positions and are indexed while they have one; hubs list up to 10 and stay `noindex` until their introductions have 150 words (D40). "All N positions" links to the filtered list appear only when more exist than are shown. The job page adds no JavaScript (the same scripts as every page).
- Review queue (M4.4, admin only): a suggested position becomes a candidate at once, in the same transaction, and the suggestion follows its candidate: in the queue until the candidate is decided, then decided, with the day its email address, link and note are deleted (90 days later, D34); reopening the candidate puts it back in the queue and clears that day. Suggestions of events, funding, papers, news, resources and other things get no candidate until their windows are built (M8–M12), so "creates a Submission and a Candidate" (§3.2) holds for positions only for now: they wait as New and editors handle them by hand and set them to Decided. An email address given without ticking the consent box is dropped before anything is stored, and the email address and note are never list columns (the list loads only its columns, so they are not sent to it either). Suggestions are created only through the local API (the form, M4.5), never through the API or the admin; editors delete one to answer a data request, which leaves its candidate and job without the link.
- A candidate starts as New, or Triaged when an editor adds it by hand with the facts. Accepting needs the address, the title as posted and the institution, is refused when a job with the same canonical address exists (the editor marks it as a duplicate of that job instead), and makes a draft job, linked from the candidate under "Job created" (`publishedRef`; a job's candidate is found by filtering candidates by `publishedRef`) and, when the candidate came from a suggestion, linked to that suggestion (`Job.submission`); the editor completes and publishes it: nothing is published from the queue. Accepting is done one candidate at a time (a bulk edit to Accepted is refused, since bulk saves run in parallel and would miss each other's jobs), and a draft made from a candidate, or marked as the job a candidate repeats, is kept (it can be edited, not deleted). Accepted is final; a rejected candidate (with a §6.1 reason; "other" needs a note) or a duplicate (of a job) can be reopened as Triaged, then decided again. The possible duplicates are found on every save: jobs with the same canonical address, jobs not closed yet at the same institution with a title at least 0.8 similar (the deadline is not compared, since a candidate has none), and other candidates with the same address unless rejected. Candidates are never deleted: they are the record of every decision (20 versions each). The admin dashboard shows what waits (new and triaged candidates, jobs in review, suggestions not decided) and the deadline tracker: published jobs closing within 14 days, the next 10 listed in the order of their deadline days. `pnpm fake:jobs` also adds seven suggestions and nine candidates covering every state. Later steps added the form (M4.5) and, with M4.7, the deletion of the source text 30 days after the decision (from the candidate and from all its saved versions, since clearing the field alone would leave the text in earlier versions that editors can read and restore), the deletion of the suggestion's personal data 90 days after the decision, and the outcome emails (`notifiedAt`). Waiting for M7: `source`, `aiSuggestion` (and the AI audit copy, D34) and candidates from source monitors.
- Jobs list (M4.3): the shared filter model is in `src/lib/filters/` (a generic part plus the jobs configuration, reused by events, funding, the map and saved searches), with the canonical form of §2.4. The proxy redirects other spellings (`308`, cached for a day; syntax only, no database), keeping `utm_*` tags after the filters so newsletter and social visits are counted. It compares queries as a form encodes them, because Next.js hands the proxy the query re-encoded (commas as `%2C`): a different spelling of the same characters is not redirected, which also rules out a redirect to itself. Values that name nothing (an unknown or retired term, a country or institution that does not exist) stay in the address, match nothing and get no chip; the chips' links and the form leave them out, so the next change drops them. Country and region are one location filter: a job in Germany or in Asia; a region stands for its countries. Deadline filters leave out rolling and until-filled positions: `7d`…`90d` close within that many days from now (`14d` is the "Closing soon" badge), `this-month` in the current calendar month (UTC), a range on its days, both included (bounded by the last moment of its last day, so it may end on 9999-12-31); the form's "Between these dates" sends both days, which the redirect turns into `YYYY-MM-DD..YYYY-MM-DD`. Days that make no range (one missing, or the first after the second) are not applied: with JavaScript, Apply shows an error tied to the date fields and moves focus there; without it, the address is not redirected and the page shows the days entered with the same error (`noindex`), the other filters applied (WCAG 3.3.1). Free text is at most 100 characters and needs every word in the title, summary or institution name (full-text search from M4.9, below). The list is one plain GET form, so it works without JavaScript; from 1280px (the header's desktop breakpoint) its filters are a bar of popovers, below it a "Filters (3)" sheet with the same controls as accordions, which JavaScript turns into a modal dialog over an inert page (a sheet opened before JavaScript ran stays in the page until it is closed, so nothing jumps when it starts; should the dialog leave the page while open, the page is released). Inside the dialog, a status region of its own announces the new count, since the page behind it is inert. Chips are links to the list without their value, so they work without JavaScript and can be opened in a new tab; the empty state's "Remove the last filter" removes the last value with a chip in the address, since the canonical address does not record the order in which filters were added; when values that name nothing are set, it offers "Remove the filters that no longer exist" instead, which drops only those. With JavaScript each change applies at once without a page load (arrow keys in the deadline options only move the choice, since they check every option they pass; Enter or Apply applies it), focus stays where it was and the new count is announced. The count and the sort order stay in place above the results, and the chips and rows are new for each address rather than moved, so a change that arrives late shifts nothing (measured with the answer held for 700 ms: at most 0.001, against 0.46 before); the results are at least a screen tall, so the footer does not rise into view. A page past the end counts its matches separately, since Payload's Postgres adapter reports none for an empty page when the query joins another table. There is no `loading.tsx`: Next.js would stream it first, and without JavaScript the page would stay a skeleton; the page arrives complete instead, and while a change loads the rows show placeholder bars in place (after 300 ms, nothing moves). When the list cannot be read, the page draws the error on the server, with the reference of the server log and a "Try again" link, so it shows without JavaScript; Next.js cannot set a page's status, so it answers `200`, not stored and `noindex`. `error.tsx` draws the same state only for failures while a change loads in the browser. No offline banner: with JavaScript a failed change falls back to a normal page load, where the browser explains that it is offline. Measured on the list: 139.4 KB of JavaScript (gzip; the list's own code 3.8 KB, and the header script about 0.7 KB more on every page for the sheet and for following the address) and 21 KB of HTML.
- Search (M4.9): `/en/search/?q=` looks through open positions, institutions and subfields; events, funding, papers, news and resources join with their windows (1d). A plain GET form, so it works without JavaScript; results come in groups with their counts, and "See all 12 positions matching …" leads to the jobs list with the same `q`. Without a query the page shows hints and the windows; when nothing matches, suggestions (spelling, fewer words, all positions, suggest a listing). It is `noindex` with `/en/search/` as its canonical address (`robots.txt` disallows it from M4.8). Positions need every word of the query in the title, the summary or the institution's name, in any of them ("postdoc karolinska"), with web search syntax ("a phrase", `-word` or `-"a phrase"`, `or`); a phrase must lie within one of the three (the document leaves a gap between them); the last word of three letters or more also finds longer words ("astro" → astrocyte). They are ranked by relevance (a word in the title above one in the institution's name above one in the summary), then by the nearest deadline. Institutions need every word that is not left out at the start of a word of their name or of one of their ROR aliases (other names, acronyms, names in other languages), and a word of three letters or digits; they are matched by at most eight words (positions by all of them), and a word that starts another is dropped, since it adds nothing ("uni university"); those with open positions come first, then active before inactive ones, then the closest names; withdrawn records never show. Subfields: active ones, by label and synonyms, with the same word rule ("glia" finds Glial biology, not "basal ganglia"). The jobs list's `q` uses the same search: its matches become `id IN (…)` (at most 5,000, far more than there are open positions; the server log says if a search ever matches more), so the other filters, the order, the pages and the counts stay as they were. Folding happens in PostgreSQL only, for the stored text and the query alike: `lower()` and then `unaccent` (the IMMUTABLE wrapper `public.f_unaccent`), so İstanbul, ISTANBUL, Istanbul and ıstanbul all find istanbul and Genève finds geneve (§12); JavaScript does not fold, since its lower case of "İ" keeps a combining dot. The words for these rules are read from the query as PostgreSQL folds it (one query of under a millisecond first), with `websearch_to_tsquery`'s own syntax, so typographic quotes and dashes (“…”, –, −) mean what `"` and `-` mean, repeats with other accents count once, and letters are counted after folding (combining marks disappear, and signs such as ① give no trigrams): a query cannot pass for three letters and make the institution search read every name. The `simple` configuration: no stemming and no stop words, as titles mix languages. A slash counts as a space ("MSc/PhD"); "Ph.D." stays one word. The migration `20260928_100000_search` (plain SQL) adds the trusted extensions `unaccent` and `pg_trgm`, which the owner of a database may create without being a superuser (the app's role owns its database on the server, `deploy/postgres/init/10-app-database.sh`), a GIN full-text index on the jobs' title and summary, and trigram GIN indexes on institution names and aliases: on the full ROR import (140,098 institutions) 12 MB and 17 MB, the whole migration in 4 s; measured on the development machine (4 cores, other work running), a distinctive name takes 2–6 ms and a very common word 0.4 s ("university", 14,604 matches; "institute", 13,625) to 1.0–1.2 s ("uni", 31,857; 0.6–0.9 s with PostgreSQL's two parallel workers), since every name the index finds is checked and ranked. That is the one search query whose work grows with the data, so the app bounds it (`src/lib/search/database.ts`): one processor (no parallel workers), at most 2 s (`statement_timeout`), at most two at a time with six waiting; beyond that, or after 2 s, the page shows the search's error state. Whatever is sent, it holds at most two of the pool's ten connections and two processors: with 12 requests for "uni" at once, repeated, `/en/jobs/` still answered in 0.09–0.25 s (before, 12 at once of the 63-character chain "uni univ … university", which took 1.5 s each, slowed it to 4.7–7.6 s). With 20,468 jobs (2,072 open, spread over the ROR institutions; tried in a transaction that was rolled back), the positions query (`rankedJobsSql`, and `matchingJobIdsSql` for the list's `q`) takes 5–13 ms for a distinctive word ("karolinska", "neuroscience"), 40–110 ms for a common one ("uni", "astro", "university", "postdoc") and 150–240 ms for a word in most positions ("research", "a"): the full-text index finds a job's own words at once, but the way in by the institution's name reads the institution of every open position, and each candidate's document is built for the check and the ranking. Both `/en/search/` and `/en/jobs/?q=` pay this on every request. Payload does not know these objects: `pnpm migrate:create` compares the collections with the last `.json` snapshot, never with the database, so it neither sees nor drops them. A restore as `deploy/backup/restore.sh` runs it (`pg_restore --no-owner --role` of the app's role, `--exit-on-error`) recreates the extensions, the function and the indexes (tried on the full data: 10 s). Down drops the indexes and the function and keeps the extensions (it cannot tell whether it installed them, and M7's duplicate check may use `pg_trgm`). The search SQL is in `src/lib/search/`, through Payload's own connection, with every value as a parameter; `pnpm test:db` runs every migration on a scratch database as a role that is not a superuser and checks folding, prefixes, ranking and the query plans (CI runs it before the end-to-end tests). Header: §2.3 ("Search (built in M4.9)"). With JavaScript, Ctrl+K (⌘K on Apple devices, where Ctrl+K edits text) and the link open a native modal dialog with the same form; Esc, its close button or a click beside it close it (not a text selection dragged out of the field), and focus returns to where it was, also to an item of a menu or filter popover, which stays open under the dialog; the page behind it does not scroll. The 404 and 410 pages carry the same form (§2.5). The dialog is drawn on the server with every page (HTML +0.9 KB gzip); opening it adds about 0.4 KB of JavaScript to every page and the rest (0.3 KB) loads on first use. Measured: `/en/` 135.61 → 136.00 KB, `/en/jobs/` 139.41 → 139.79 KB (0.21 KB below the budget, so the next addition to every page must save as much elsewhere), `/en/search/` 136.00 KB. Waiting: live suggestions in the dialog (a combobox over a JSON route with the same queries, debounced, with the count announced; they would load with the dialog, not with every page), the other windows (1d), a Turkish search page (with the Turkish site) and a rate limit for `/en/search/` (Caddy, on the pre-launch checklist, D42; it needs a plugin and a custom Caddy build, `deploy/README.md`, and would be extra protection: the limits above already bound the cost).
- The server loads Payload from `node_modules` instead of bundling it (`serverExternalPackages` in `next.config.ts`, as Payload already does in development): the production build had copied Payload into each part of the server, and refused saves then reached the admin without the message at each field. An end-to-end test signs in as a test editor (`e2e-editor@example.org`, added by `pnpm e2e:editor` in CI and locally, never on the production server) and checks those messages.
- Sitemaps, jobs feed and robots.txt (M4.8): `/sitemap.xml` is an index of the files under `/sitemaps/` that list at least one page: `pages.xml` (the home page, the unfiltered jobs list, the content pages that are no longer drafts, today only About, and the subfield index once one of its hubs is indexed), `jobs.xml` (open jobs; `lastmod` is when the job was last saved), `institutions.xml` (institutions with at least one open job) and `subfields.xml` (active hubs whose written introduction has at least 150 words, an unset active flag counting as active, as in the proxy; none yet). A file exists only while it lists a page, since the sitemap schema asks for at least one URL: a type with none (today `subfields.xml`) answers `404` and is left out of the index. A page's robots rule and its sitemap entry come from the same function (`src/lib/indexing.ts`), so a page is listed exactly when it is indexed; an end-to-end test fetches every listed URL and checks `200`, no `noindex` and a canonical link to itself. With that rule, a hub is indexed once its English introduction has at least 150 words (§2.2, §16.3, D40; hubs exist only under `/en/subfields/`, so the Turkish text of the field does not count), and the subfield index with the first such hub. `countWords()` in `src/lib/indexing.ts` counts them: a word is a run of letters or digits in any script (Turkish ı, İ, ş, ğ included), kept whole across an inner apostrophe or hyphen ("Türkiye'nin", "well-known") and across `.` or `,` between digits ("3.5", "1,000"); punctuation, symbols and emoji alone are no words, other punctuation separates words (so "e.g." or a URL counts one word per run of letters or digits), and white space and blank lines between paragraphs do not matter. The admin shows the count below the field ("87 words: 63 more words before search engines list this hub"; with Turkish selected, the count and a note that only the English introduction decides), and the field's description states the rule. A file holds at most 50,000 URLs (`jobs-2.xml` and so on, only when needed) and one locale (`pages-tr.xml` once Turkish pages are indexed, §12).
