The file right after a type's last one in a locale repeats that file's last URL, so a file listed by an index read shortly before still answers after jobs close (the URL that was there has moved back into the file before); other names of sitemap files answer a plain `404`, and names no sitemap file can have get the 404 page from the proxy, like any unknown URL (both not stored). `lastmod` is written only where the last change is known: for jobs (last saved) and content pages; not for the home page, the lists, institution pages and hubs (they list open positions with the days left, which change as deadlines pass, and the date of the job saved last would move back in time when it closes), nor for the entries of the index (a file also changes when a page leaves it, which its pages' dates cannot show); hreflang alternates are those of the pages. The files are read from the database on each request, may be kept for an hour (`Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600`), carry `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` (the files themselves stay out of search results; their URLs are still read) and answer `503` with `Retry-After` while the database is unreachable, like the proxy. `/en/jobs/feed.xml` is RSS 2.0 of the 50 open jobs published last: the original title, a link to the job page, `pubDate` from the first publication, and our summary with the institution, place and deadline day as plain text (never text from the posting). Its `guid` is the job's short address (`/en/jobs/{shortId}/`, which redirects to the current one), so a corrected title does not repeat the item in feed readers. It has no `lastBuildDate` (optional in RSS 2.0), for the same reason as the index: the channel also changes when a job leaves it. Same cache, `X-Robots-Tag` and `503`; linked from the jobs list (`<link rel="alternate">`); not in the sitemaps. `robots.txt` disallows `/admin`, `/api`, `/en/search/` and every address with a query string (`Disallow: /*?`, understood by Google and Bing): filtered and later list pages name the unfiltered list as canonical, and every page to index is in the sitemaps.

- Suggest a listing (M4.5): `/en/suggest/` (indexed, listed in the sitemap) and `/en/suggest/sent/` (`noindex`; reaching it directly changes nothing). The form asks for the address of the original page, the content type (radio buttons, none chosen in advance, so an event is not sent as a position by mistake), an optional note and an optional email address with an unticked box "Email me the outcome of this suggestion"; the hint next to the box says what the privacy texts say (only for the outcome, deleted 90 days after the decision, 16 or older) and links to the privacy policy and the KVKK notice, and does not call it "açık rıza" while B8 is open. An email address without the ticked box, or the box without an address, is an error the person corrects; nothing is dropped silently. Every check runs on the server (zod, `src/lib/forms/`) and every message comes from `messages/*.json`, so the form works the same without JavaScript (a Server Action with `useActionState`; without JavaScript the browser posts the form and the page comes back with the messages); only the spam check needs JavaScript, which a `<noscript>` note says, with `hello@gliagora.com` as the alternative. The address must be http or https, of a named host, without a user name or password, at most 2,000 characters (also in the canonical form); internationalised domain names are fine. It is stored as typed, so it may not contain what the URL parser would silently drop or change (spaces, tabs, backslashes, control and text-reordering characters); the collections refuse these too (`isHttpUrl`). The note is plain text, at most 500 characters, without control or text-reordering characters. After an error, the summary at the top (links to the fields) takes focus, the fields keep what was typed and carry `aria-invalid` and their message; the note's count is in the server's HTML (so nothing moves when the scripts arrive), updated as the person types and read out only within 50 characters of the limit. After a suggestion is taken, the Server Action calls `refresh()` before leading to the sent page, so Back shows the form with a new challenge rather than the router's cached copy with the one just used.
- Spam protection (M4.5): ALTCHA's first proof-of-work scheme (`altcha-lib/v1`, MIT, no network calls): each page gets a signed challenge (HMAC-SHA256 with `ALTCHA_HMAC_KEY`, valid 21 hours: over the 20 hours after which WCAG 2.2.1 sees no time limit, under the 24 hours used answers are kept; a secret number up to 50,000), and a small solver of our own (about 2 KB with the form; the ALTCHA widget would not fit the 140 KB budget and adds interface we do not need) finds it with `crypto.subtle` in batches of 500 when the form is first used, giving the browser a turn after each batch (`scheduler.yield()`, or a timer where it does not exist), since Chromium settles the hashes without one and the search would otherwise block input and painting: about 0.3 s on average on a desktop and 1.1 s with the processor slowed down four times, with no task over about 70 ms (an end-to-end test checks this). A form sent before the scripts have run is answered that the page had not finished loading or JavaScript is off. The server checks the answer, and each answer sends one suggestion. Without `ALTCHA_HMAC_KEY` the page says the form is temporarily unavailable and the rest of the site works (the production Compose file requires it). The hidden field (`reference_code`, a name browsers do not fill in automatically) answers as if it worked and stores nothing. The checks run cheapest first: the fields, the hidden field, the spam check, then the rate limit (5 suggestions per hour per address), so mistakes in the form and failed spam checks count against nobody. The limit and the used answers are rows in the hidden `form-limits` collection (no admin, no API), decided in one PostgreSQL transaction with an advisory lock per key, so parallel requests cannot exceed it: the address is stored only as an HMAC-SHA256 with a key derived from `PAYLOAD_SECRET` (IPv6 by its /64 network), read from the first `X-Forwarded-For` entry that Caddy sets (for IPv6 visitors only because the production Compose network has IPv6 enabled: otherwise Docker forwards them from its bridge gateway and they would share one limit; `deploy/README.md` step 5 checks it before any `AAAA` record is published); rows are deleted within 24 hours (D34): whenever a suggestion passes the spam check (whatever the limit decides), and by the hourly purge (M4.7). They never go into backups: `deploy/backup/backup.sh` dumps the table without its rows (`--exclude-table-data`), so a restore starts with empty limits. A database error shows "Something went wrong" with a reference, and the log records only the reference, the error's class and the database code (read from the error's causes, since Drizzle wraps the driver's error; never a message, which quotes the values sent). `/en/suggest/` loads 137 KB of JavaScript. The outcome emails, the 90-day deletion and the hourly purge of `form-limits` came with M4.7 (the purge deletes rows older than 23 hours, so none outlives 24 hours between runs, which is safe because the limit needs one hour of rows and a challenge is valid 21 hours). B8 (the KVKK basis for the optional email) stays open.

- Report a problem (M4.6): `/en/report/` and `/en/report/sent/`, both `noindex` (§2.2). Every job page links to the form as `/en/report/?item=job:{shortId}`, also once the job has closed, and every institution page, after its facts as on a job page, as `/en/report/?item=institution:{id}`, with the ID its address ends with (the ROR ID, or the short ID of an institution added by hand; D40); the notice and takedown page links to it without an item. The item is shown ("About:" and the title or name, linked) only when it names a job whose page is public (open, or closed less than 30 days ago) or an institution that exists; a draft, a job in review, a job answering 410, an unknown institution or a malformed value is ignored, and the form works as a general report. The item is checked again when the report is stored, which then links the job or the institution and keeps the page's address. About an institution the form offers only the kinds that fit it (a wrong fact, a broken link, personal rights, something else), with hints about the institution's facts and website; "closed" and "not from the employer" are for jobs only, and the server refuses them for an institution item with a message at the field (the collection refuses them too). A general report and a job offer every kind, as before (D40). The form asks what is wrong (a wrong fact, closed, a broken link, not from the employer, personal rights, something else; each with a short hint, personal rights explained in plain words with Law 5651 Art. 9), the details (required, plain text, at most 2,000 characters; the hint asks for no sensitive data, as the notice and takedown page does) and an email address: optional, but required for a personal-rights request, so that we can answer; the hint says it is used only to answer about the report and kept with it for 2 years after it is closed, as the privacy texts say. There is no consent box: the privacy texts base these requests on a legal duty and the protection of rights (KVKK 5(2)(ç), (e)) or on legitimate interest, not on consent. The form reuses the M4.5 parts, now shared: `src/lib/forms/public-form.ts` (the fields, the hidden field, ALTCHA, the rate limit, the messages) and `src/components/forms/` (the spam check, the error summary, the fields). Its limit is its own: 5 reports an hour per hashed IP address, counted apart from suggestions (keys `ip:report:…` and `ip:suggest:…` in `form-limits`, deleted within 24 hours: D34, amended on 2026-09-28 to name both forms, as the privacy texts do); a spam-check answer sends one form, whichever it is. The sent page says what happens next for a personal-rights request (confirmed within 24 hours, usually decided within 72 hours) and for other reports (checked against the original posting, then corrected or removed; a reply only if an email address was given), and does not say which kind was sent. About an institution, the form's introduction and the acknowledgement email say instead that an editor checks the institution's own website and, where there is one, its ROR record; the sent page does not know the item and keeps the general wording ("the original posting") for the operator to review (D40). Measured on the built app: `/en/report/` loads 138.1 KB of JavaScript (gzip; the form's own code 2.5 KB) and `/en/suggest/` 138.3 KB (2.7 KB) with the shared parts, against 135.6 KB for a page without a form.
- Reports are stored in the `reports` collection (admin "Reports and requests", in the Review queue group; the `TakedownRequest` of §5.1): `kind`, `job` or `institution` (only from a checked item; the admin shows the one a report names in the same place, D40), `itemUrl`, `details`, `email`, `locale`, `status` (New, In progress, Closed; never back to New; closing needs an `action`: no change needed, corrected, removed, refused, not applicable), `actionNote` (editors only), `createdAt` (shown as Received), `respondBy` (a personal-rights request 24 hours after it arrived, §3.2, §15.1, §15.4; other reports 30 days, the KVKK countdown of §3.2), `closedAt`, `purgeAt` (2 years after closing, §18.1) and `ackSentAt`. Reports are created only through the local API by the form; what the person sent is read-only, also for code that bypasses access control; the email address, the details and the note are never list columns and are not loaded by the list; signed-in editors delete a report to answer a data request. The kind stays as the person chose it: an editor who sees that a report is really a personal-rights request answers it within 24 hours all the same. Reports keep no versions, so the 2-year deletion leaves no copies; if versions are ever switched on, that deletion must remove the `_reports_v` rows too. The admin dashboard shows the new reports and the personal-rights requests nobody has answered yet that are due within 24 hours, with the overdue ones. Deleting the item clears the link (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) and the report keeps `itemUrl`: a job at the retention deletion (M4.7), an institution added by hand when an editor deletes it (reports do not keep an institution). `pnpm fake:jobs` adds six reports in every status, one of them about an institution, and removes those the end-to-end tests send. The automatic acknowledgement email (sets `ackSentAt`; only when an email address was given, so always for personal-rights requests) and deleting reports 2 years after they were closed (`purgeAt`) came with M4.7. Waiting for M4b: data requests (the data-request page and its verification), which will look for reports by email address as well.

- Scheduled work (M4.7; built and tested locally, D38: nothing runs before the server exists, and only where `PAYLOAD_JOBS_AUTORUN` is `true`): six Payload tasks in a `maintenance` queue, which the app's scheduler ticks every minute (the ROR import keeps its own queue, ticked every 10 minutes at second 30, so that the two ticks never write Payload's record of the last scheduled runs at the same moment and undo each other's; `src/jobs/autorun.ts`); times are UTC. `expireListings` (hourly at :05) sets published jobs whose fixed deadline has ended to Expired through the collection (a normal save: `expiredAt`, the two cleared checks, a version), each in a transaction that locks the job and checks it again, so an editor's change a moment earlier wins; deadline days end on the hour or at a quarter or half past it (:15 in Kathmandu, Eucla and Chatham; :30 in India, Iran, Newfoundland and a few others), so a job is marked within 50 minutes (§2.5: within an hour; a unit test checks every time zone). `purgeFormLimits` (hourly at :05) deletes form-limit rows older than 23 hours. `syncJobPlaces` (04:45, after the ROR import at 03:30) saves again the jobs whose institution moved, so their country and city follow (the M4.1 open point); jobs with a complete location of their own are left alone. `purgeRetention` (04:15) and the two email tasks, `notifySubmitters` (every 15 minutes) and `acknowledgeReports` (every 5 minutes), are described below. Each task is idempotent, works in bounded batches, runs one at a time (a PostgreSQL advisory lock, also against a manual run), logs only counts and IDs, and stores a failure with the error's class and code only, never its message (PostgreSQL, Drizzle and mail servers quote values and addresses in theirs); the next scheduled run is the retry. At startup, jobs that a restart left marked as processing, in every queue, are released: they would block their task's next scheduled run. A maintenance job left marked as processing while the app kept running (PostgreSQL restarted during a run, so Payload could not record its end) is released at the next tick of its task, when no run holds the task's lock. `pnpm jobs:run [task …]` runs the tasks once, in a queue the scheduler never runs.
- The retention task (D34, §18.1) deletes this and nothing else. The source text of a candidate, 30 days after the decision, in the candidate and in all its saved versions (`_candidates_v`), in one transaction. A suggestion whose `emailPurgeAt` has passed (90 days after the decision) is deleted as a whole, with its email address, link and note (its `url` is required, so it cannot stay without the link); its candidate keeps the decision. For a candidate from it that was rejected or marked as a duplicate, the suggested link and title (and the canonical address and title fingerprint made from them) are cleared in the candidate and its versions; an accepted candidate keeps its address, which is the employer's page we published (the job's `originalUrl`), while the listing exists; status, reason, decision date and links stay. A candidate keeps a hidden mark that it came from a suggestion (`fromSuggestion`), so the same happens 90 days after the decision when an editor deleted the suggestion earlier (a data request), whichever came first. An admin form opened before the purge would send the link and title back on save, and no later run would clear them again: the collection keeps them cleared until the candidate is reopened. A listing that has answered `410` for 12 months (withdrawn, or 30 days after it closed; calendar months in UTC) is deleted with its versions, and its short ID, last slug and the time go into `retired-listings` (admin: Listings → Retired listings, read-only): the proxy answers `410` for those IDs (unknown IDs stay `404`), and a new job never gets a retired short ID. The jobs collection refuses the deletion except with the retention context flag (a symbol no request can send), and even then only for a job that is due and whose address is already kept; the candidate the job was made from and the candidates marked as its duplicate keep their decision, but lose the posting's address and title (with the canonical address and fingerprint), also in their versions, in the same transaction, so that only the address and ID remain (D34, the privacy texts); reports that pointed to the job keep their records; and the database clears the links (`ON DELETE SET NULL`). The facts accepting needs are checked only on the save that accepts, so such an accepted candidate can still be saved. A closed report is deleted once `purgeAt` (2 years after closing) has passed. Backups keep earlier copies for up to 6 months, as the privacy texts say; after a restore, the next run deletes again whatever is due.
- Emails (M4.7): `@payloadcms/email-nodemailer` sends through the SMTP server in `SMTP_HOST` (with `SMTP_PORT`, `SMTP_SECURE`, `SMTP_USER`, `SMTP_PASS`, `EMAIL_FROM`; STARTTLS is required except on this computer). In production this is the SMTP server of the Turkish mailbox set up at M2 (no separate relay at the start: §11.6, §11.7.1, D22); until then `SMTP_HOST` stays empty in production: the tasks send nothing and read no addresses, and Payload's own emails (a new admin password) are dropped with a log line that names neither the recipient nor the subject (Payload's own fallback would log the recipient). With a mail server, every send error is replaced by one that carries only its codes, since Nodemailer's name the refused recipients and Payload logs its own failed emails whole. Messages are plain text (nothing that could track opening), from `messages/{locale}.json` (`emails`) in the language of the form, from and replying to hello@gliagora.com, marked as automatic (`Auto-Submitted`). The outcome email goes once to those who ticked the box: "not added", with a plain reason for each decision (never the editor's note), or "published", with the job's address, once the accepted candidate's job is public; an accepted suggestion waits until then, and if its data is deleted first (90 days), no email is sent. A "not added" email goes out only once the decision has stood for an hour, so an editor can reopen a mistaken rejection first, and each suggestion is read again, locked, just before its email goes out, so a reopened decision, a cleared address or a deleted suggestion wins. Spam gets none (the address may not be the sender's), and nor do suggestions of other kinds, decided by hand, until their windows record an outcome (M8–M12). The email repeats the link the person sent and says that we delete the email address, link and note 90 days after the decision and that it is the only email about the suggestion. The acknowledgement goes to each report with an email address that is not closed and was received within 7 days (so nobody gets one weeks later, after an outage or when email is first set up); it names only the Gliagora page the report is about, and for a personal-rights request says that our editor answers within 24 hours and that we usually decide within 72 hours. `notifiedAt` and `ackSentAt` are set only after the mail server has accepted the message, and database triggers keep them set against a save that read the record a moment earlier (Payload writes the whole row); when the server cannot be reached, the run stops and the next one tries again (a message is sent twice only if the app stops between sending it and recording it). Locally, Mailpit (`docker compose --profile mail up -d mailpit`, on 127.0.0.1 only) catches every message; `pnpm test:db` runs the tasks against a scratch database and a throwaway Mailpit container, also in CI.
- Layout robustness (M4.10): `tests/e2e/pseudo-locale.spec.ts` (§12) turns the text of a page into a pseudo-locale in the browser (accented letters with the Turkish İ, ı, ş and ğ; each text 40% longer and marked with `[ ]`; numbers and addresses unchanged; the lockup, the brand in every language, marked `translate="no"`, stays) and checks at 320×640 and 1280×800 the home page with the menu open, the jobs list (chips, rows, pages; the popover on desktop, the sheet on a phone), a job page, the suggest and report forms with their errors, search results and an institution page: no sideways scroll, no text cut off or outside buttons, chips, badges, labels and the layers that stay in place, a header that keeps its height, and no focused control under the sticky header or the action bar, walking the Tab order forward and backward (as Shift+Tab does). Screenshots go into the test report for a person to look at; they are not compared with stored baselines, since fonts and rendering differ between machines. `layout.spec.ts` checks every template at 320px with text at 200% (WCAG 1.4.4; the root size doubled, which media queries do not see), with the search form's button in one piece, and the search dialog at that size; and with the browser's own text size enlarged (Chromium's default font size, which rem media queries follow) the desktop header at 24px on a 1920px window and 32px on 2560px, and no focused control under the header at 32px on 1024px. The page's sideways scroll is measured against the width of the layout (the `<html>` box), since `clientWidth` includes the 15px that `scrollbar-gutter: stable` keeps and so hid up to 15px of overflow. What they found, and the fixes (CSS and tokens only): buttons wrap a label that does not fit rather than overflow; the header's lockup gives way first when the header needs room (by container queries, which see the text size: it drops the descriptor, then the name; both stay in the link's name), which fits the header at 200% on a 320px screen (M4.2's finding) and labels 40% longer at 1280px; and never gets narrower than its mark, so the link, and its focus outline, encloses the mark; the mark is sized in px; long words and addresses break inside the page and the footer (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`; tables keep their words and scroll); the gutters, and the search dialog's padding, stay within 5% of the screen width; the search form's button moves below the field where the field would keep less than 6rem, and in the dialog a label word that does not fit beside the close button moves below it; the page frame is 80rem (1280px at the default text size), so it grows with the browser's text size as the breakpoints do; while focus is in the content, the strip under the sticky header counts as out of view (`scroll-padding-top` on `<html>`, only then, since focus in the header would otherwise scroll the page), so Shift+Tab, or Tab from one footer column to the next with enlarged text, no longer leaves a focused control under the header (WCAG 2.4.11). At normal size the desktop header is pixel-identical to before (English and Turkish, 1024–1440px); at 768px with a desktop scrollbar the digest button now stays on one line. With the browser's own text size at 200% (its default font size) no template scrolls sideways from 320 to 1440px, since the rem media queries follow it, and the page frame follows it too, so the desktop header fits its frame from 80rem at any text size (before, at 24px on a 1920px window it ran about 200px past the content below it); with only the root size doubled, the header still overflows at 768px and from 1280px, where the full navigation shows.
- Fonts (M4.10): the fallback faces have bold faces: a regular face drawn bold keeps the regular widths, while bold Atkinson is 7% wider, so bold labels and buttons re-wrapped when the fonts arrived; Commissioner's fallback is Arial Bold at 94.9%. The home heading's width is in em (9.3em), not ch, which changed with the font. Layout shift with the web fonts 1.5 s late (`layout.spec.ts`, now also the home page, the jobs list, a job page and search results; at most 0.1, the §17 target: M4.10 set 0.05, which proved too tight on CI, where the title of `/tr/iletisim/` fills its line at 412px within a fraction of a pixel and takes a second line in the fallback, 0.058; the failure message lists what moved and which font faces loaded): the jobs list at 412px 0.076 → 0.035 (one heading line that fits within 0.3px in one font and not the other), the home page at 1280px 0.033 → 0.0003, the privacy policy 0.013, every other template tested below 0.01.
- JavaScript at the end of M4 (first load, gzip, as `budgets.spec.ts` prints it): 135.54 KB on every page without a form (before M4.10 136.00), `/en/suggest/` 138.20 (138.65), `/en/report/` 138.07 (138.53), `/en/jobs/` 139.34 (139.79), the 404 page 135.29. The saving (0.46 KB on every page): the language switcher, a client component because its links follow the address, gets its two icons from the server, so their definitions and lucide's `createLucideIcon` leave its client code. Also found, not taken: lucide's `Icon` component is marked `"use client"` in lucide-react, so every lucide icon a server component draws is a client reference to it, and it is sent with the header script on every page (about 1.0 KB gzip); drawing the site's icons as plain SVG on the server would save that with no feature lost. Also not taken: the region names in the list's filter configuration (about 0.1 KB, used only by the admin). CSS 8.2 KB and fonts 68.4 KB on every template; HTML at most 36.4 KB (privacy policy), 22.1 KB for the jobs list.

**Access audit (after M4, 2026-09-28).** After the M4.1 institutions bug, every collection's and global's access rules were audited (REST, GraphQL, the auth routes, the admin, the pages). Signed out, REST refused every read and write, and no page showed an unpublished record or personal data. Found and fixed:
- (medium) Caddy's second password guarded only `/admin`: Payload's sign-in under `/api` (REST and GraphQL) skipped it, so the editor's password alone opened the whole API, and after the first deploy anyone could create the first account at `/api/users/first-register` before the operator did. Caddy now guards `/admin` and `/api` (`deploy/Caddyfile`, kept by a unit test); `deploy/README.md` step 5 checks for "Create first user" and the `401`.
- (medium) Nothing kept the site private until M2 (D32): Caddy now asks for the same password on every path except `/health/` (§18.2, "Private phase"), and only that one path: `/health` and `/health/` reach the app as `/health/`, while anything below it (which would show the 404 page with the site's navigation) asks for the password.
- (low) GraphQL was on although nothing uses it (field-name suggestions, several sign-ins in one request, errors in the log): it is off, and `/api/graphql/` answers `404`.
- (low) The rule for a suggestion's status did not check the user, so Payload's permission routes told signed-out requests which suggestions have a candidate. It does now, and every collection and global states each rule (`src/lib/access.ts`); the scheduled tasks can no longer be started through `/api/payload-jobs/run` by any signed-in user (the scheduler and `pnpm jobs:run` use the local API).
- (hardening) At most one password-reset email per account every 15 minutes (Payload's default: 15 seconds); `serverURL` from `SITE_DOMAIN` in production, so an editor's session cookie is accepted only from the site's own origin and reset links are absolute; reset tokens and `/api` query strings are left out of the access log, also from the `Location` and `Refresh` headers of Next.js's trailing-slash redirect, and the image sets `NEXT_TRAILING_SLASH`, so Payload's own links (reset emails, sign-in redirects) end with the slash and are not redirected; failed searches and lists (and the feed, sitemaps, health check and proxy) log the error's class and code, not a message that quotes the words searched for.
- Not changed: Payload answers a request to restore a version of a collection without versions with `500` and an error in the log (it reads the missing table before checking access); behind the `/api` gate only editors reach it. Deleting the last editor account would reopen the first-account route, also behind the gate.
- `tests/e2e/access.spec.ts` now checks all of this signed out, for every collection and global (§18.2, "Access rules"). Besides the newest records it tries one in each state that a rule for single records could treat differently (every status of jobs, candidates, suggestions and reports; suggestions without a candidate and of each kind; an institution added by hand, which `pnpm fake:jobs` now adds, with the draft job at it), and each must exist in the test data. It takes the collections and globals from `src/payload-types.ts`, which CI now regenerates and compares. At S: check through Caddy that the admin signs in, lists and saves (the browser reuses the second password for `/api`; the admin's session is its cookie, and a script cannot send `Authorization: JWT` through the gate, since that header carries the password) and that a signed-out `POST /api/users/login/` answers `401`; Caddy's rate limits for the auth routes are decided then (D23). The end-to-end tests sign in as the test editor once per run and share the session (`tests/e2e/editor.ts`): sign-ins at the same moment in parallel workers overwrite each other's session in the user record.

**M4 summary (2026-09-28).** Built and tested locally with fake data only (D38): the Job collection with deadlines in their time zones, closing and `410`; the review queue (suggestions, candidates, duplicate warnings, dashboard and deadline tracker); job pages; the jobs list with the shared filter model, chips, the filter sheet and pages; "Suggest a listing" and "Report a problem" with ALTCHA and rate limits; the reports collection; sitemaps, the jobs feed and `robots.txt`; search in PostgreSQL; layout checks for longer languages, larger text and late fonts; the scheduled work (the hourly expiry, the form-limit and retention purges, retired listings that keep answering `410`, the outcome and acknowledgement emails; it runs only on the production server and sends email only once SMTP is set). Waiting for S: the first deploy, real content entry, the search rate limit in Caddy, the IPv6 check before any `AAAA` record, and the checks on the deployed site (pre-launch checklist below). Waiting for M2: the SMTP settings of the Turkish mailbox for the emails (D22: no separate relay at the start), and the end of the private phase (D32). The jobs window stays private at M2, when only the coming-soon page, the newsletter signup and the legal pages go public, and goes public at the M6 launch, once the §3.4 gate is met (D39). Waiting for M4b: data requests and accounts; M5: the map view of the list; M7: sources and candidates from monitors. Numbers: 927 unit tests in 87 files, 43 database tests and 544 end-to-end tests pass on the desktop and phone projects (362 more are skipped where a test is written for the other project), on 31 templates; the budgets hold on every template (JavaScript above: at most 139.34 of 140 KB).

**Operator decisions on the M4 open points (2026-09-28).** The operator approved M4 on 2026-09-28 and decided:
1. Subfield hubs: a hub is indexed (robots, sitemap, subfield index) only when its introduction has at least 150 words (§16.3), not as soon as any introduction is written (D40; built in a follow-up step before S).
2. The jobs feed's `guid` stays the job's short address (`/en/jobs/{shortId}/`), which stays when a title changes (D40).
3. The jobs list keeps the chips below the count (so a change shifts nothing), a deliberate difference from the §9.3 sketch (D40).
4. No `loading.tsx` skeleton and no offline banner: the reading of §3.2 as built in M4.3 stays (D40).
5. D34 now names both public forms: hashed IP addresses for the rate limits of the suggestion and report forms are kept 24 hours, as the privacy texts already say (D34, amended).
6. Institution pages get a "Report a problem" link, like job pages and the notice and takedown page (D40; follow-up step before S).
7. A job's deadline time zone is prefilled from its institution's location (ROR gives coordinates, not a zone), and the editor can still change it; built now, since content entry starts right after S (D40; follow-up step before S).
8. At M2 only the coming-soon page, the newsletter signup and the legal pages go public; jobs and the other sections stay private until the §3.4 public-launch gate is met (at least 150 active verified positions across at least 25 countries), that is, the M6 launch (D39).
9. The Turkish interface strings added so far (`messages/tr.json`, drafts) are collected in one table in `docs/tr-strings-review.md` (key, English, Turkish, where it appears) for one review pass by a native speaker before the Turkish site (§12) (D40; follow-up step before S).

**Still open or scheduled (M4).**
- Live search suggestions in the search dialog: later (M4.9 sketches them).
- A rate limit for `/en/search/` in Caddy: on the pre-launch checklist with the other Caddy limits (D42; it needs a plugin and a custom Caddy build). Until then the app's own limits bound the cost of search.
- The IPv6 check (`deploy/README.md` step 5): done at S and passed (S notes below). The `AAAA` record was already published before S.
- B8 (the KVKK basis for the optional email on the suggest form) stays open for the lawyer review.
- How the private sections stay private while the M2 pages are public (D39): set up at M2. D41 decides what is public then (the coming-soon page, the newsletter signup, the About page and the approved legal texts) and how those pages link (a reduced header and footer; email in place of the forms). Built switched off (D50, 2026-10-02): the setting `SITE_STAGE` and Caddy's list of public paths (§18.2), the M2 versions of the public pages (below, §2.2) and the steps of the switch (`deploy/README.md` section 11).

**S notes (2026-09-30).** The one-time server setup, run by Claude Code from the operator's Linux computer with the operator's SSH key, following `deploy/README.md` (decisions: D41 for M2, D42 for S).

- **Server:** Hosting Türkiye Micro Paket (KVM, Istanbul), host name `vps-4448`; Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS, kernel 6.8.0-142; 3 vCPU, 3.8 GiB RAM plus a 2 GiB swap file, 38 GB disk; IPv4 `45.144.214.78/24`, IPv6 `2a0f:85c1:7f5:6e::1/64`; the provider sets the network's MTU to 1400, and Docker's networks now use it too (`setup-server.sh`), since Docker's 1500 can make TLS from containers hang; time zone UTC. The provider's cloud-init sets Google's DNS resolvers (8.8.8.8); the server looks up only fixed names (GHCR, Docker Hub, Ubuntu, Let's Encrypt, `zenodo.org`, `api.ror.org`), never anything a visitor typed.
- **DNS** (Cloudflare, DNS only, set by the operator): `A` and `AAAA` for `gliagora.com`, `www` as a `CNAME`. No `CAA` record yet and DNSSEC off (§11.6; the operator's to add).
- **SSH and accounts:** before S, password login was on: the provider's `50-cloud-init.conf` says `yes` and is read before `60-cloudimg-settings.conf`. `00-gliagora.conf` is now read first: no root login, no passwords, only `gliagora` (the operator's two keys, copied from root) and `deploy` (the deploy key only, restricted to `deploy-remote.sh` through one sudo rule; no shell, terminal or forwarding, all checked). The provider's two files are left as they were. Host key ED25519 `SHA256:2Xl/K1bL4dalAwaHVZKLQjv7Kx2mNSV5nOGlO+FCCzk`, pinned in the secret `DEPLOY_KNOWN_HOSTS`. The firewall rate-limits SSH (6 new connections in 30 seconds per address); fail2ban banned the first scanning addresses within minutes.
- **Software:** Docker Engine 29.8.1, Compose 5.5.1, restic 0.16.4, OpenSSH 9.6p1; automatic security updates with a reboot at 01:30 UTC when needed (the server was rebooted once after the setup for the new kernel; everything came back by itself).
- **Stack:** Caddy 2.11.4, the app `sha-8ba4332`, PostgreSQL 18.6; about 150 MB of memory for the three containers when idle. Let's Encrypt certificates for `gliagora.com` and `www.gliagora.com` on the first try (TLS 1.3, renewed by Caddy). First deploy through the Deploy workflow on 2026-09-29 at 23:32 UTC (about a minute); the server keeps no registry login.
- **IPv6 check (step 5): passed.** Over IPv6 and IPv4, Caddy's access log shows the visitor's own address (masked to /48 and /24), not a Docker address, so the form rate limits and the logs see real visitors.
- **Checks from outside (IPv4 and IPv6):**
  - HTTPS works for both names;
  - http redirects to https, and `www` to `gliagora.com` (308, path and query kept);
  - HSTS is set, and HTTP/3 answers;
  - only TCP 22, 80 and 443 are open (all 65,535 ports scanned; everything else is dropped), and UDP 443 serves HTTP/3;
  - signed out, every path answers `401` from Caddy except `/health/` and its spellings, which return only `{"status":"ok"}`: pages, feeds, sitemaps, `robots.txt`, static files, the admin, every `/api` route (sign-in, first account, password reset) and the 404 and 410 pages;
  - signed in, 23 templates (the admin too) load in light and dark mode with no request to another origin and no CSP report, and the CSP allows only `'self'`.
- **Backups:** the first backup and a restore test passed (41 tables, 15 migrations, in a temporary container without network). The nightly backup (00:30 UTC) and the monthly restore test (the 1st, 02:00 UTC) are switched on. The site-wide password and the backup password were generated on the server into files only root can read (`/root/site-password`, `/opt/gliagora/backup/restic-password`); the operator reads them from their own terminal (`deploy/README.md`).
- **Scheduled jobs:** running (`PAYLOAD_JOBS_AUTORUN`): the maintenance tasks on their schedules, without email until the M2 mailbox (`SMTP_HOST` empty); the first ROR import runs at 03:30 UTC on 2026-09-30 and loads all institutions.
- **Two incidents during S:**
  - The first site-wide password was shown once in the Claude session: curl's `%{redirect_url}` repeats the credentials of the request. It was replaced within minutes, before any backup held it, and the old one is refused. `deploy/README.md` now warns about this.
  - A full-speed scan of all ports from the operator's own connection made the provider's network block that IPv4 address for about 20 minutes; IPv6 kept working. `deploy/README.md` now warns about this too.
- **CI:** three tests failed now and then on 2026-09-29, one of them turning `main` red. The search dialog's axe check (it measured during the fade-in) and the search database test's teardown (a closing connection ended by the database drop) are fixed (#45). The phone late-font check of the jobs list is held back (#47): its title fills its line in the web font itself (pre-launch checklist).
- **Left for the operator:**
  - save both passwords in the password manager;
  - create the first editor account (`/admin/`);
  - start the weekly copy of the backups to their computer;
  - add a `CAA` record and switch on DNSSEC in Cloudflare (optional);
  - decide whether deploys become automatic after merges (manual for now, §11.5).

**M5 notes (2026-10-01).** The map (PLAN §14), in four steps, each tested locally on Node 24 and PostgreSQL 18 with the ROR sample and the fake jobs, as in CI.

- **M5.1, the basemap** (#52):
  - `deploy/basemap/fetch-basemap.sh` puts on the server the zoom-10 extract of the Protomaps planet (OpenStreetMap data; 3.8 GB for the build of 2026-09-30) and the fonts (SIL OFL) and sprites (MIT) of its style. Every download is checked against pinned values, and the files go into versioned folders.
  - `/basemap/current.json` names the current version, so a refresh needs no deploy and can be rolled back.
  - Caddy serves `/basemap/` (range requests, versioned files cached for good, behind the password until M6).
  - The tests use a 2.3 MB Istanbul fixture of the same layout.
  - `docs/vendors.md` now lists every external service.
- **M5.2, the world map** (#53):
  - `/api/map/institutions/?{filters}` answers GeoJSON with the panel's text in words, kept 10 minutes. The list and the map resolve the filters with the same code, so they always count the same positions.
  - `/en/map/`: MapLibre GL JS 6.11.2, loaded natively from `/map/maplibre-gl/<version>/` (copied at build time, not bundled). Its worker therefore comes from our origin and shares MapLibre's code with the page, and the site's CSP allows it.
  - The style is `@protomaps/basemaps` with our tokens, in light and dark. Clusters and pins show their counts.
  - The panel (side panel, or bottom sheet on phones) takes focus, and Esc gives it back.
  - "Institutions in view" is the keyboard and screen-reader way in, with "Skip the map".
  - Without WebGL or JavaScript the page is the list. There is no static preview image: a picture made earlier would not show the current pins (§14, "Fallback").
- **M5.3, the jobs list's map view** (#54): `?view=map` puts the list and the map side by side with the same filters (§14, "Sync"). The map component loads lazily (`LazyMapView`); `next/dynamic` did not keep it off the plain list.
- **Measured** (`tests/e2e/budgets.spec.ts`, the map templates have their own budget):
  - the map page: 458.6 KB of JavaScript, of which MapLibre with its worker is 307.8 KB;
  - the map's own code: about 323 KB against PLAN's unverified 280 KB, so §17.3 now says ≤330 KB (approved, D45);
  - the jobs list: 139.91 of 140 KB. Before the next change to the list's own client code, find savings there first (done after M5: 136.33 KB, §17.3, D45).
- **Tests:** 1044 unit, 48 database (8 email tests need Docker locally) and 602 end-to-end tests pass on 34 templates. The map tests load MapLibre, its worker and the basemap under the exact CSP of `deploy/Caddyfile`, with nothing from other domains.
- **The basemap on the server** (2026-10-01, with the operator's go-ahead):
  - the new `Caddyfile`, `compose.prod.yml` and `deploy/basemap/` were copied (the files checked against `main`, the Caddyfile validated by Caddy);
  - `fetch-basemap.sh` downloaded build 20260930 in 3 minutes (3.8 GB, `pmtiles verify` passed, the fonts and sprites matched their digest; 27 GB still free), and `update.sh` restarted Caddy with the new route after a backup.
  - **Checked from outside:** `/basemap/` answers 401 without the password. With a temporary second user (a random password that never left the server unpiped, removed afterwards), `current.json` was `no-cache` and the tiles answered 206 to range requests. In Chromium, the world map drew the basemap with its labels, MapLibre and its worker loaded from our origin, nothing came from other domains, and there was no CSP violation under the real policy; on desktop and on a phone the credits read "MapLibre | Protomaps | © OpenStreetMap contributors", under the map too.
  - **Backups:** the operator asked that the basemap stay out of the backups and the weekly copy. `backup/backup.sh` names its files one by one, so the snapshots hold no basemap file; the repository is 19 MB. It now says so in the script and the runbook.
  - Events on the map come with M8.

**M6 notes (2026-10-01; the home page, built ahead of the lawyer review and M2 at the operator's request, D45).** The steps after M5 first, then the home page; each tested locally on Node 24 and PostgreSQL 18 with the ROR sample and the fake jobs, as in CI, and each reviewed before merging by independent reviewers whose findings were checked by others.

- **The M5 decisions** (#56): D45; the OpenStreetMap credit (map data and the time zones of deadlines) on the About page, in a new "Data and credits" section with ROR, Protomaps and MapLibre, as well as in the terms; B20 stays open.
- **The jobs list's savings pass** (#57, D45 point 4):
  - the site's seven icons are drawn on the server as plain SVG (`src/components/Icon.tsx`, Lucide's shapes), and `lucide-react` is gone: −0.96 KB on every page;
  - the list's behaviour without page loads (`ListBehaviour`) loads when the reader first reaches for the filters, chips or sort order; what they do before it arrives is kept and done, and if it cannot load (a broken connection, or a deploy since the page was opened) that action loads its page, as without JavaScript;
  - JavaScript: every page 135.54 → 134.58 KB, the jobs list 139.90 → 136.33 KB (136.48 KB after M6), now with a budget of its own, ≤137 KB.
  - **The ≤130 KB of D45 cannot be reached**: the chunks that hold only React, Next.js 16.3 and Turbopack's runtime weigh 131.84 KB on every page (a webpack build was no smaller). The operator decided (D47): ordinary pages keep ≤140 KB and the jobs list keeps its cap of ≤137 KB (§17.3).
- **The editor guide in the admin** (#58): `/admin/help/`, "Yardım" at the end of the side menu, rendering `docs/editor-guide-tr.md` (kept in the image and the server build).
- **The home page** (#59), at `/en/` in place of the coming-soon page:
  - the hero: the tagline as the H1 (the LCP element), the subline, "Find positions" and "Explore the map";
  - the static poster (§10, L1): an inline SVG of 7.3 KB, the brain from the side with its folds drawn as contours of its outline, and one calcium wave drawn in once in 1.2 s (not with reduced motion; the system's colours in forced-colours mode). Four drafts in different styles (line art, points, an astrocyte, contours) were drawn and judged side by side for anatomy, polish and accessibility; the contour draft won and was refined. It is generated by `tools/poster`;
  - the windows that are built (`LIVE_WINDOWS`: Jobs until M8) as links around the brain, with their counts;
  - four live figures (open positions, deadlines this month, institutions, countries), each equal to the number on the list its link opens, kept for 10 minutes and read again when the month changes;
  - the latest positions and the next deadlines (six each, four on phones), read with each request;
  - "Where is your next lab?" with the countries as links (no picture of the map, D45);
  - the windows overview (Jobs, with its three largest position types);
  - Glia Notes near the hero and before the footer, leading to `/en/newsletter/` until the signup opens (M2).
- **Measured** (`budgets.spec.ts`): the home page loads 134.58 KB of JavaScript (none of its own), 10.13 KB of CSS and 19.71 KB of HTML; the poster is 7.3 KB of the 15 KB allowed.
- **Tests:** 1049 unit tests, and 632 end-to-end tests locally; CI runs both, and now restarts the app after loading its test data, since the smoke test reads the home page from the empty database and the figures are kept for 10 minutes.
- **On the server:** deployed by itself after the merge, behind the password. Until positions are published there, its figures read 0 and its lists say so.
- **Still to come for M6**, the launch itself, when the operator says so: the §3.4 gate and the pre-launch checklist below; the full header and footer, and the forms' links in place of email (D41); the launch issue of Glia Notes; opening the map. At M2, before that, the public `/en/` shows the coming-soon text again (M2 row).

**M4b notes (2026-10-02; member accounts and self-service privacy, built ahead of the lawyer review and M2 at the operator's request, D48; the design is D49).** Seven pull requests (#64–#70; these notes came with #71), each tested locally on Node 24 and PostgreSQL 18 with the ROR sample, the fake jobs, the fake Google and ORCID and Mailpit, as in CI, and each reviewed before merging, with the review points fixed in the same pull request. Production said "Signing in is not available yet" throughout. Before them, in the same task: #62 (CI minutes, D46; the session handoff in `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/STATUS.md`) and #63 (D47, D48; the Turkish review starts with the strings that are public at M2).

- **The collections and the sign-in core** (#64; nothing visible):
  - `members`, `member-sessions`, `email-tokens`, `saved-items` and `deletion-log`: hidden, `nobody` for every operation, no API routes; `data-requests` for editors (admin "Data requests", in the Review queue group; editors never delete one). A raw-SQL migration adds the constraints that guard what the SQL store writes (at least one way in; the address lowercased);
  - `src/lib/members/`: the configuration (each way of signing in switches on by itself once its settings exist), tokens and hashes, the sealed sign-in cookie, return paths, addresses, ORCID iDs, `appOrigin()` and the origin check, the identity rules (`resolve()`, one decision table) and the SQL store;
  - form limits with several keys (per IP; per hashed address, one budget shared by every form that sends an email; site-wide), public-form Server Actions that refuse forwarded calls, and `sendOne()`, which reports the mail server's reply codes;
  - a job's saved items go with the job; every deletion of a suggestion or a report by an editor or a data request is logged for restores (the retention task's are not: it repeats itself after a restore); backups leave out sessions and email links (`backup.sh`); the sign-in variables in `compose.prod.yml` and `deploy/.env.example`.
- **Email-link sign-in, the account frame and sign-out** (#65): the sign-in page with the email form (spam check, limits); the link bound to the browser that asked for it (a warning in another browser); the emailed link's page only shows, and its button POSTs; sign-out here or everywhere; the account page with its sub-navigation, and the sign-in panel in its place when signed out; the header's Account link; the robots rules. The rest of the deploy files, so that the operator copies them once, together with #64's: Caddy's log filters in one snippet for both its access log and its error log; `restore.sh` carrying the deletion log and the data requests over a restore and moving the ID sequences past them (and removing its test container's volume); `deploy/README.md` step 9 ("Switching on member sign-in"). CI: the smoke checks, Mailpit and the end-to-end app on the host's network, `pnpm e2e:member` (§11.5); the Mailpit program for `pnpm test:db` where Docker is missing.
- **Google and ORCID** (#66): `oauth4webapi` 3.8.8 (`src/lib/members/oidc.ts`): state, a nonce that is always required, PKCE where the provider's discovery advertises it (Google does, ORCID does not), and the ID token's signature checked; start and callback routes; "Create your Gliagora account" (`/en/sign-in/confirm/`, with the unticked box for the ORCID name); linking and unlinking in the account, which signs the member's other browsers out. The fake providers (`tests/fake-oidc/server.ts`, `pnpm fake:oidc`, with a fixed test key) cover every fault the code checks; `docs/vendors.md` lists Google and ORCID sign-in.
- **Saved positions** (#67): Save on the jobs list and job pages (a link to signing in when signed out, a POST form when signed in, a stub in the header script that loads the rest on first use); `/en/account/saved/` (open positions first, then those no longer open, which can be removed there); saving across sign-in, with the position named on every page on the way.
- **Settings** (#68): the digest (an unticked consent box; the date of the consent text is stored), the preferred subfields (D35, with `prefs=off`, §2.4), the display name, and adding or changing the address with a 24-hour link that only the member who asked can use.
- **Export, deletion and the member tasks** (#69): "Download my data" (JSON), "Sign out everywhere", the deletion's confirmation page and the page after it; the tasks `sendRequestEmails` (every 5 minutes), `cleanInactiveMembers` (05:30 UTC), `purgeMemberAuth` (hourly at :10) and `applyDeletionLog` (every 10 minutes), and the new purges of `purgeRetention` (closed requests after 2 years, unconfirmed ones after 60 days, the deletion log after 7 months).
- **The data-request form** (#70): the form at the end of `/en/legal/data-requests/` (email, kind, optional name, details); the minimal record and the 24-hour link; the link's page, its confirmation and the automatic deletion, with the email after it; a database guard that keeps an unconfirmed record free of anything else (migration `20261002_090000`); `deploy/README.md` step 10 (finding an unconfirmed request from an address); the M4b facts in the legal drafts (privacy policy, KVKK notice, terms, data requests; the cookies draft named the two member cookies with #65; all still drafts, each lawyer point marked); the end-to-end tests on two CI workers.
- **Measured** (`budgets.spec.ts`, JavaScript, gzip): ordinary pages 134.70 KB (134.58 before; the Save stub is 0.12 KB); the jobs list 136.60 KB of its 137 KB cap, signed out and as a member, with the preferred subfields applied or with `prefs=off`; the sign-in, account, saved, privacy and deletion pages 136.96 KB (the spam check of the sign-in panel or of the address form); the data-request page 137.30 KB (like the suggest and report forms, 137.37 and 137.24 KB). CSS 10.22 KB on every template but the two map views (20.53 KB with MapLibre's stylesheet); HTML at most 40.01 KB (the privacy policy, which grew with the M4b facts), 24.55 KB for the jobs list (CI of #70).
- **Tests:** 1592 unit tests in 132 files, 162 database tests (the member store, the inactivity timeline, the data-request deletions, the deletion log after a restore, the migrations down and up) and 985 end-to-end tests locally (605 more skipped where a test is written for the other project), on 62 templates, 28 of them new with M4b; CI runs them all (§11.5, with the `destructive` project last).
- **On the server:** each pull request deployed itself after its merge, behind the password; the four new migrations ran, and the new tables are empty. Sign-in stays switched off, as D48 asks: the server's `.env` has no Google or ORCID keys, `SMTP_HOST` stays empty until the M2 mailbox, and the M4b deploy files are not on the server yet (copying them changes the server, so it waits for the operator's go-ahead; the older copies do no harm while sign-in is off and the tables are empty, and they must be replaced before any key or `SMTP_HOST` is set). So the app's start line reads `Member sign-in: email off, google off, orcid off`; the sign-in and account pages say "Signing in is not available yet" and give privacy@gliagora.com; the jobs list has no Save buttons; the data-request form says it is temporarily unavailable; and the member tasks run with nothing to do (without email nobody is warned, so nobody with an address could be deleted for inactivity).
- **Open points:**
  - D49 was approved by the operator on 2026-10-02 as built, with each point below, and one addition: the account page tells a member without an email address that we cannot warn them before an inactivity deletion and that adding an address lets us (and lets them join Glia Notes), as the privacy drafts do (built with D50). Where the design changes PLAN's text: the static Account link; the confirmation of data requests; what a confirmed deletion also removes; no email after a deletion for inactivity, and members without an email address deleted without warning; accounts never merged once they exist; the deletion's confirmation page instead of a dialog; the Google sign-in app in "Testing" until the launch; the data-request form public at M2 with its approved page.
  - For M2: should deleting an account for inactivity also end its digest subscription? Answered by the operator on 2026-10-02: no; the subscription keeps its own consent record and unsubscribe link (§13; §22.2, question 11, resolved).
  - The lawyer review (`docs/legal-open-questions.md`, "Added with M4b"): M4b added points to B4 (the basis for the 60-day record of unconfirmed requests), B5 (whether a request through the form needs more than an optional name), B9 (accepting the terms on the "Create account" step), B11 (60 days for unconfirmed requests; whether a confirmed deletion should also clear the address on personal-rights requests) and B13 (how to describe Google and ORCID sign-in). Every legal text stays a draft.
  - Real sign-in: the pre-launch checklist below (the Google and ORCID clients, the deploy files first, the ORCID gate, the Google consent screen at M6).
  - Waiting for the M2 mailbox: email-link sign-in; adding an address (so members who sign in only with ORCID cannot join the digest yet); the data-request form; the emails after a deletion; the inactivity warnings, and with them every deletion for inactivity of a member with an address; and the M2 items of the milestone table (the listmonk sync, `ConsentLog`, `eraseNewsletter()`, the email cap, Umami's setting, the log filters first).
  - Not in M4b: Save on the home page, institution pages, hubs, search and "similar positions" (they stay cacheable; each a small addition later); saving events (M8) and programs (M9); saved searches and alerts (M8). The account pages are English only; their Turkish strings are drafts in `docs/tr-strings-review.md`, not public at M2.

**M2 notes (from 2026-10-02; the technical parts behind the password, D50).** Built and tested locally on Node 24 and PostgreSQL 18 with fake data, a real listmonk (the release binary where Docker is missing) and Mailpit, and in CI with the pinned images; nothing public, no real email.

- **The server's parts** (listmonk, backups, logs, alerts, the email cap; `deploy/README.md` sections 11 and 12):
  - listmonk v6.2.0, unmodified, pinned by digest, always on but needed by nothing (the app and its deploys work while it is down), with its own database user and database (`deploy/postgres/init/20-listmonk-database.sh`, also run on the existing volume), published on the server's loopback only; its admin through an SSH tunnel. `deploy/listmonk/prepare.sh` (run by `update.sh` before the stack starts) installs it once with its admin, whose password is generated into a root-only file and reaches the install on stdin only, and applies `deploy/listmonk/settings.sql` from the repository: the samples removed, the private single-opt-in list Glia Notes (the site runs the double opt-in, so listmonk only ever holds confirmed subscribers), the app's API user (subscribers of Glia Notes only, found by ID or by address, never by an SQL expression: listmonk 6.2's `subscribers:sql_query` lets a function such as `query_to_xml` read every table of its database, so the user does not have it; only the token's SHA-256 stored) and the settings (no update check, no public pages, no tracking or IP addresses, none of its own unsubscribe headers, no captcha services, no bounce processing yet, the mail server from the app's `SMTP_*`), each only where it differs, then a restart when anything changed. listmonk treats role 1 as its super admin whatever it holds, so the file refuses to run before the admin's install made role 1 (otherwise the app's role would be made first, as role 1). `pnpm listmonk:setup`, the tests and CI apply the same file; CI applies it with the server's own psql input and runs listmonk with the server's hardening (no capabilities, no new privileges). Its admin's links use the tunnel's address (`http://localhost:9001`).
  - Backups: `DATABASES="gliagora listmonk"`; a database the server has but the list lacks fails the backup after the others are stored, and so does a listed one that was backed up before and is gone (one never backed up is skipped: listmonk before its first start); listmonk's admin sessions are left out; the monthly restore test checks each database in its own container (listmonk's by its `migrations` setting); `restore.sh database … listmonk` stops and starts listmonk, carries nothing over and puts the current settings back; restore both databases from the same night.
  - Deploys: only the app's image is pulled; a deploy whose app is healthy is not rolled back when another service is not (it names the service); when listmonk cannot be prepared (its database, upgrade or settings), the update leaves it as it is, starts everything else and fails naming it, so app deploys never wait for listmonk; going back changes only `APP_IMAGE` in `.env`.
  - Container logs: every service logs to the systemd journal, which keeps 14 days (`MaxRetentionSec=14day`, daily files, at most 1 GB) with no copy for syslog (D34); `docker compose logs` works as before. PostgreSQL logs errors without their DETAIL and without the failed statement (`log_error_verbosity=terse`, `log_min_error_statement=panic`), so a duplicate address from listmonk or the app never reaches the journal; its health check runs over TCP, never against the temporary server of a new volume's set-up. SSH and fail2ban lines in the journal keep full IP addresses (14 days, for security). Open point: kernel lines (the firewall's blocked packets, unmasked addresses) still reach `/var/log/ufw.log` and `kern.log` through rsyslog, and fail2ban's own ban lines `/var/log/fail2ban.log` (Ubuntu's default log target; logrotate keeps four weekly files), both about four weeks; for the lawyer review (B11) and before the launch, decide whether to move them into the journal (fail2ban's `logtarget = SYSTEMD-JOURNAL`, rsyslog's kernel lines) or name them in the privacy texts.
  - Backup emails (D42): `deploy/backup/alert.sh` after every run of both units: every failure, the Sunday backup's and the restore test's success; through the app's mail server (STARTTLS required), to `ALERT_EMAIL`, with the run's own log lines and no address; off until both are set.
  - The cap on the forms' emails is a setting (`EMAIL_CAP_HOUR`, `EMAIL_CAP_DAY`; default 50 and 200; the start line names it), to be set from the mailbox's limit at M2 with room for the scheduled emails and Glia Notes (its confirmation links count; its issues and welcome emails do not, and a rush of signups sends about as many welcomes as links). It never goes above a value set: the hour is held to the day (also to the default day), and an unreadable value becomes the stricter of its default and the other setting, with an error line.
- **Glia Notes** (`src/lib/newsletter/`, `src/lib/forms/newsletter*.ts`, the pages under `/en/newsletter/`; §9.10, §13). It switches on by itself, as sign-in does (D48): unsubscribing, and the deletion log's part in listmonk, with `LISTMONK_URL` (the app gets `http://listmonk:9000` from `compose.prod.yml`) and `LISTMONK_API_TOKEN`; the signup also with `SMTP_HOST` and the spam check, so on the server it stays off until the mailbox (start line `Glia Notes: signup off (no mail server), unsubscribe on`); without them the page says signing up is not open yet (it offers no subscription by email, which would skip the double opt-in). The code does not check that the legal texts are approved: the newsletter pages and routes join M2's public list only once the açık rıza metni is (`draft: false`, `src/lib/public-pages.ts`; the form's links to the privacy policy and the KVKK notice keep only their words while those are drafts, and the reduced header and the coming-soon page offer Glia Notes only while its signup is public); until then only the team signs up, behind the password.
  - Double opt-in run by the site, so listmonk holds only confirmed subscribers: the form (an address, the unticked box with the account's words, the minimum age and the privacy texts in its hint, the spam check, 20 an hour per IP address (a campus network or a conference shares one), at most one email a minute per address, the forms' shared email budget; the same answer for every address) stores a 7-day `newsletter` email link (the address, its language, the consent text's version and the time of the form) and emails it while the person waits; "Check your inbox" shows the address masked from a sealed cookie (an hour from the form, never extended, also holding the form's time and version) with "Send it again", whose link records the form's consent, not a new one. The link's page asks with a button; confirming consumes the link, makes the address listmonk's confirmed subscriber of Glia Notes (an address listmonk has already is added to the list again; listmonk is asked once more when its answer was lost, since it may have acted), writes the consent records and ends the address's other links (skipping one that a concurrent confirmation holds), in one transaction; if the records cannot be written, a subscriber the confirmation made is deleted again in listmonk, so the link still works and listmonk holds nobody without consent records. The welcome email (after the commit, best effort) says the first issue comes with the launch, gives the unsubscribe link and carries `List-Unsubscribe` with RFC 8058's one-click; the confirmed page says it is on its way, never that it arrived. The app's calls to listmonk go through one client (its own API user, 5 seconds, no cookie, never an SQL expression, errors without listmonk's answers): by ID, by address, or the list of Glia Notes read page by page.
  - The consent log (`consent-log`, §5.1): listmonk's subscriber UUID and ID, the event, the consent text's version (`GLIA_NOTES_CONSENT_VERSION`, the date of the açık rıza metni, also used by the account's setting now), the time and the source; no address; constraints in the database; kept 3 years after the subscription ended (`purgeRetention`), carried over a restore of the app's database like the deletion log.
  - Unsubscribing takes effect at once, without signing in or JavaScript: from the page's button or a mail program's one-click POST (the one POST route without an origin check: the subscriber's UUID, in the emails only, is the credential; the link also names its ID, which the app looks up, then compares the UUID in constant time); it writes the consent record and a deletion-log row (`newsletter`, listmonk's subscriber ID) and ends the address's pending signup links, then deletes the subscriber in listmonk. An unknown ID, another UUID or listmonk's preview UUID change nothing and get the same answer, which says the address is deleted "if it was on the list"; an incomplete link shows a page that says so.
  - Every 10 minutes `applyDeletionLog` reads the list of Glia Notes whole (pages of 500; about 2 seconds for 10,000 subscribers) and, so no logged row waits behind others, checks every listed subscriber: one whose ID the deletion log names is deleted again (a failed deletion, or a restore of listmonk's database) when it was created no later than the logged deletion and has not confirmed a signup since; and a subscription whose newest consent record is not an unsubscribe, while listmonk answers by its ID that it has no such subscriber, gets an `unsubscribed` record with the source `listmonk`, so its records are deleted 3 years later instead of never. Subscribers are added and deleted only through the site, never in listmonk's admin (`deploy/README.md` section 11).
  - Caddy removes the query strings of every address under `/en/newsletter` from its logs (the link's token, a subscriber's ID and UUID); the server's `compose.prod.yml` (listmonk's address for the app), `Caddyfile` and `backup/restore.sh` (which carries the consent log over a restore) need copying, with the operator's go-ahead, before `SMTP_HOST` is set (`deploy/README.md` section 14).
  - Points for review (D50): the welcome email is sent once, while the person waits, and is not repeated if the mail server refuses it (the subscription stands; signing up again sends it again); listmonk commits a new subscriber before the app's transaction does: a failure of the app's records deletes it again, but a failure of the commit itself, or a lost answer from listmonk followed by a failure of the second call, can leave a confirmed subscriber without its consent records until the person uses the link again (which still works); "Send it again" sends a new link and leaves the earlier ones working until one is used; the unsubscribe route has no rate limit (a mail provider's servers must reach it; guessing a UUID is not practical); an address listmonk has outside Glia Notes (only an admin could make one) makes the confirmation fail, and its unsubscribe link answers "failed"; the deletion log's check reads at most 200,000 subscribers per run (then it warns, and ends no subscription that run), deletes at most 1,000 again and looks up at most 500 missing ones; the signup's per-IP limit (20 an hour) lets one address use more of the forms' shared cap; the welcome emails do not count against the cap.
- **Members' Glia Notes and erasure** (`src/lib/newsletter/members.ts`, `src/lib/newsletter/erase.ts`; §13, §18.1):
  - The members' sync (`syncGliaNotes`, every 5 minutes at :02, :07, …; once right after the settings route stored a change, an address link verified an address or a sign-in link made an account, after the answer, `syncSoon`): each member whose choice waits (`digest_changed_at` set and `digest_synced_at` empty or older; every change of the choice, and an address change while on, empties it) in a transaction that holds the row (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`). On: the subscriber with the address, else the member's own subscriber (`digest_subscriber_id` and `_uuid`, both or neither; looked up by ID, UUID compared) moved to the new address, else a new one, made a confirmed subscriber; a subscriber it replaces is deleted (logged, `unsubscribed` with the source `account`); the member's consent (`requested` and `confirmed`, with the stored version and where and when it was given: `digest_consent_source` and `digest_requested_at`, the account's settings at the time of the choice or the newsletter page's form and confirmation) is recorded under the target unless its subscription has a confirmation at that time or later and no unsubscribe after it, so an address change alone records nothing and a member subscribed anew after a restore of listmonk gets the records under the new subscriber as the person gave them. Off: the address's subscriber and the member's are deleted (logged, `unsubscribed`) and the address's pending signup links end. listmonk's deletions follow the commit (best effort; the deletion log repeats them; their log rows are dated after everything the transaction read, so a subscriber made or confirmed while the sync waited for the row is deleted again). A stored subscriber that another member's row names is not the member's (never moved or deleted); a confirmed signup that reuses a subscriber releases it from a member whose address moved away before the sync (`releaseDigestSubscriber`). A newly proven address (`digest_adopt_check_at`, set by `createMember` with an address and by `applyChanges` on a new address) is looked for once: a subscription listmonk has for it in Glia Notes, with its consent on record and named by no other member, is taken over by a member with Glia Notes off and no subscriber (the newsletter page's version, source and times; no new records); otherwise the mark is cleared. The run stops when listmonk cannot answer (unreachable, timeout, unreadable or 5xx) or the database fails, and goes on after a refusal for one member (a 4xx).
  - The daily `checkGliaNotesMembers` (06:00 UTC) reads the list of Glia Notes whole and asks by ID for each member's subscriber it does not show; one listmonk no longer has (or whose ID now has another UUID) makes the member's choice wait again, and so does one listed under another address than the member's (a restore of listmonk from before an address change). One whose newest consent record is a withdrawal on the site (`unsubscribed`, any source but `listmonk`) at or after the row's choice is switched off instead (`endDigestByWithdrawal`, no records, no listmonk call); the sync does the same for a waiting member whose stored subscriber is gone. Members the deletion log names are left to `applyDeletionLog`. Nothing is marked when the list cannot be read whole, or is empty while members hold subscribers.
  - The newsletter routes keep members in step in their transactions: a confirmed signup at a member's address switches Glia Notes on (synced, with the form's version and subscriber; a member whose row names another subscriber keeps it and waits, so the sync deletes it), an unsubscribe of the address or the member's subscriber switches it off.
  - `eraseNewsletter()` is real, in the callers' transaction (account deletion on purpose, now with the member's row read again and locked; a confirmed deletion request): the address's pending signup links end; the address's subscriber and the member's own are logged (`newsletter`) and their consent records unlinked (UUID and ID cleared, `updated_at` left alone, logged as `consent-log`/`unlink`; also the records of a member's subscriber that listmonk no longer has); after the commit the subscribers are deleted in listmonk (best effort). listmonk unreachable fails the whole deletion ("try again"; the data-request link still works), and so does listmonk not being configured while the member's row names a subscriber or the newsletter has records (consent records or logged subscribers): an erasure never goes ahead blind. An end of a subscription noted afterwards (`recordEndedSubscriptions`) is written only while a linked record of it remains, so it never names an erased subscriber again. The account deletion's record stores `newsletterRemoved`, and its final email says when a subscription ended. `applyDeletionLog` unlinks again a logged consent record that names a subscriber again and was created no later than the logged unlink. Deleting an account for inactivity leaves Glia Notes alone (§22.2, question 11; an integration test checks that the subscriber and its records stay).
  - The export ("Download my data") adds the consent records of the subscription the account holds (event, version, source, time; never the UUID or ID) and where and when the consent behind "on" was given; its schema stays `gliagora-export/1` (new keys only). Records of subscriptions that had ended before are not in it.
  - `restore.sh` carries deletion-log values a restored snapshot does not know (`consent-log`, `unlink` in a snapshot from before this deploy): it loads the rows into a table whose enum columns are text, checks the labels first and stops before changing anything, so `restore.sh carry` lets the app's migrations add them.
  - The account page tells a member without an email address that no warning comes before the deletion for inactivity (24 months and 30 days, from `src/lib/retention/rules.ts`) and that adding an address lets us warn them and lets them join Glia Notes, with a link to the address form when it is offered (the operator's addition to D49). The account texts that mention Glia Notes on a deletion, and the inactivity warning, say that an inactivity deletion leaves Glia Notes and a deletion on purpose ends it.
  - The legal drafts (all still drafts) describe what was built: the pending signup in the app's database for 7 days (not in listmonk, not in backups), listmonk with confirmed subscribers only and no tracking or IP address, the consent record and its unlinking, the welcome email, the unsubscribe (a page with one button and the mail program's one click, the address deleted at once; the account's switch, within minutes; the link's ID and UUID), the newsletter cookie, the newsletter links' query strings removed from Caddy's logs, the containers' logs (14 days in the journal), members' Glia Notes, and the no-address notice; the açık rıza metni's version is its date (`GLIA_NOTES_CONSENT_VERSION`, now 2026-10-02 with this edit). Sentences with a link to the suggest or report form read well with the form and with email (M2's links).
  - Points for review (D50): the members' sync takes over a subscription the member's new address already had through the form (the form's records stand when they are as recent as the member's choice, otherwise the member's consent is recorded under it); a member's subscription moves to a new verified address without new consent (lawyer question B19); an account created for an address that is already subscribed through the form, or a member who adds such an address, takes that subscription over within minutes (Glia Notes shows on, with the form's records; B19), also when the member had switched Glia Notes off earlier for another address; a member's "off" deletes the subscription of the member's address even when it was made through the form; after a restore of the main database, a member whose consent records show a withdrawal after the row's choice is switched off, and so is a member whose subscriber an address change replaced after the snapshot (the safe side; B11); members get no welcome email when they switch Glia Notes on in the account; erasing waits for listmonk (a deletion fails while it is unreachable, or not configured once the newsletter has records), while the deletion in listmonk follows the commit; bounce and complaint removals (M6) must end subscriptions through the site, or the daily check adds members back; a subscriber listmonk holds outside Glia Notes (only an admin could make one) is neither moved, deleted nor unlinked by the site; the daily check reads at most 200,000 subscribers and looks up at most 2,000 missing ones per run.
- **The M2 state, prepared and switched off** (`src/lib/site-stage.ts`, `src/lib/public-pages.ts`, the twins under `src/app/(frontend)/m2/`, `deploy/caddy/m2-public.caddy`; §2.2, §11.5, §16.1, §18.2; `deploy/README.md` section 13):
  - One setting, `SITE_STAGE` (`private` by default, or `m2`), which Compose passes unchanged to Caddy and to the app; any other value keeps the app private and stops Caddy from loading. The pages are built once in CI, so the setting reaches static pages through the proxy: every public page also exists as a static twin with the reduced header and footer, and in the M2 state the proxy (which reads the setting with each request) shows the twin in its place.
  - One list of public pages feeds the proxy, the M2 sitemaps and `robots.txt`, and Caddy's file (`pnpm caddy:public`; a test fails when it is stale): the coming-soon `/en/`, About, each legal text only once approved (`draft: false`) with the pages that belong to it (the data-request form's with its text, the newsletter's with the açık rıza metni), the machine files and the assets. Caddy opens each address only in its exact spelling, answers the app's 404s and 410s with a plain "Not Found", and Caddy and the app mark each other's public requests and answers, so a copy of the list that is ahead of the running image opens nothing more.
  - In the twins, links to the suggest and report forms become `mailto:hello@gliagora.com` and links to private pages keep only their words (D41); `docs/m2-links-review.md` (`pnpm m2:links`) lists each such sentence for the operator to read before approving a text.
  - Tested in CI with the production Caddyfile in front of a second container of the same image (`tests/e2e/m2.spec.ts`), in both stages and with every text approved.
