  - Points for review (D50): twins with a proxy rewrite, in place of dynamic pages (one forgotten page would show the full header in public) or a second build; the language button only where both pages of a translation are public; "Write to us at hello@gliagora.com" at the foot of the page while Contact is a draft; a menu only on phones; the newsletter public only once the açık rıza metni is approved (the header's Glia Notes button and the coming-soon page's paragraph appear with it); the footer's "About" heading over a single link while Contact is a draft; 0.13 KB more JavaScript on every page (the jobs list is 0.26 KB below its cap).
- **The task's record** (2026-10-02 to 2026-10-05; `docs/NEXT.md`): #73 (the operator's M4b decisions, D50 opened), #74 (the server's parts), #75 (the M2 state), #76 (Glia Notes), #77 (members and erasure, the legal drafts), #78 (the journal's file name, these notes). Each code pull request was built in its own working copy, checked locally (lint, type check, unit, database and end-to-end tests, the build) and reviewed by four independent reviewers whose findings were each checked by another before being fixed: 24, 21, 29 and 36 confirmed findings, all fixed before merging. Among them: listmonk's `subscribers:sql_query` permission letting the app's token read any listmonk table (not granted; listmonk has not been told, the operator decides); the full-site 404 page reachable without the password in the M2 state through other spellings of public addresses; link tokens in a response header that Caddy logged; a restore subscribing members again who had withdrawn. Numbers at the end: 1,840 unit tests in 151 files, 229 database tests, 1,080 end-to-end tests (643 more skipped where a test is written for another project). CI: about 205 of the month's 2,000 minutes (19–27 for each pull request's run, 6–7 for each push to `main`, 2 for each deploy; two runs were repeated for one admin test, the job form's time zone: under load the answer to one change of the form could arrive after the next one's and show a field again, so the test now waits for the form's requests to end before each change and makes the choice again if the field has not followed, and the M2 tests run after the others).
- **On the server** (2026-10-05, with the operator's go-ahead for these steps; `deploy/README.md` sections 9, 7, 12 and 14):
  - The M4b deploy files first (as they were at #73), `update.sh --no-pull`, Caddy restarted: its logs record `/en/sign-in/?[removed]`.
  - The two unnamed volumes that earlier restore tests had left (2026-09-29 and 2026-10-01, a copy of the database each) were removed; `restore.sh test latest` then passed and left none.
  - This task's files: the two new secrets generated on the server (never shown), `DATABASES="gliagora listmonk"` and an empty `ALERT_EMAIL`, the journal's limit, the units' alert line, then `update.sh --no-pull`: listmonk v6.2.0 installed (its admin's password only in `/root/listmonk-admin-password`), its settings applied from the repository, four services healthy. Checked: listmonk answers only on the server's loopback (nothing from outside; its admin through the tunnel), the app reaches it inside the stack, its API user sees only Glia Notes and is refused settings and SQL expressions; the backup stores both databases and the restore test restores both ("Restore tests passed: gliagora listmonk."); every container logs to the journal; the app's start lines read `Glia Notes: signup off (no mail server), unsubscribe on` and `Site stage: private`; every address but `/health/` still asks for the password, also those the M2 state would open; Caddy's logs record `/en/newsletter/confirm/?[removed]`.
  - Found there and fixed in #78: Ubuntu ships its own journal setting (`/usr/lib/systemd/journald.conf.d/syslog.conf`, `ForwardToSyslog=yes`), which was read after our file and kept a copy of every line in `/var/log/syslog`; the file is now `zz-gliagora.conf`, read last, and the installer checks the settings in force. The firewall limits new SSH connections (six in 30 seconds): several copies in a row are refused for a moment.

**Notes on the pre-launch technical items (2026-10-07; `docs/NEXT.md`, D51).** Built and tested locally on Node 24 and PostgreSQL 18 with fake data, and in CI; nothing public, no email sent. Five pull requests, each checked locally first (lint, type check, unit and end-to-end tests); the limits, the server check and the Lighthouse script were then reviewed by two independent reviewers, whose findings were checked and fixed (#83, and in #84 before it was merged).

- **Two legal drafts** (#80; §15.4): the accessibility statement (`/en/legal/accessibility/`) and the sources and editorial policy (`/en/about/sources/`), in English, both drafts, listed in `src/lib/content.ts`, linked in the footer as §2.3 says, with their M2 versions and a line each in the M2 list of public pages.
  - The statement names WCAG 2.1 AA (§17.1) and describes the tests as they are. A survey on 2026-10-07 with axe-core's WCAG 2.0, 2.1 and 2.2 A and AA rules and its best practices found no violation of any severity on any template (light and dark, desktop and phone); axe-core could not measure the contrast of a few texts (the poster's labels, the map's credits, cells of a wide table on a phone), which the statement says. Its known limitations: no screen-reader walkthrough and no outside audit yet, forced-colours mode only partly tested, the map, the forms' spam check needing JavaScript, links that expire, other websites.
  - The policy gives today's automation rule (§6.1) and says that no program reads a source of positions and no AI tool drafts items today.
  - Open points, named in the drafts' markers: C6 to C12 for the operator and B21, B22 and additions to B1 and B17 for the lawyer (`docs/legal-open-questions.md`). The About page (approved) is unchanged.
- **The server check** (#81; §18.2, "Monitoring"; `deploy/README.md` section 15): `deploy/monitor/check.sh` with its timer. The limits (85% of a file system, 10% of the memory, 10% of five minutes waiting for memory, a sixth of a certificate's lifetime) are at the top of the script. A report is the unit's failure (exit 1), which `backup/alert.sh` emails as "server check found a problem" ("failed" for exit 2, the check itself, once a day at most). Tested against stand-ins for `df` and `openssl`. After the review (#83): what it has seen is kept under `/run`, in memory (on the disk it watches, a full disk left a problem "seen once" for ever); a problem that comes and goes is one problem (it was reported every 45 minutes, or never); a report counts only once `alert.sh` says that its email has gone (a refused email meant a day of silence); a limit that is no whole number is refused (it switched its check off).
- **Rate limits in Caddy** (#82, #83; §18.2; `deploy/README.md` section 16): the image (§11.5), the four zones, the `429` answer and the tests.
  - After the review (#83): the jobs list and the map's data count with any query (Caddy's query matcher is false for a query it cannot parse, a stray `%` or `;`, which the app still searches for); the counts run over 15 or 30 seconds, records are cleared every 10 seconds and the container has a memory limit (the limiter's record per address, before the password, let one IPv6 /48 take Caddy from 61 to about 900 MB; now about 355 MB); HTTP/3 early data is off; the admin's first-account page is not counted (its form asks the server after every change); the image's workflow builds only when the registry says the tag is missing; the runbook switches Caddy alone, after a backup.
  - Its cost: with an answer of its own for the `429`, Caddy no longer writes the reason for a `502` into its own log (only at the debug level); the access log keeps each one.
  - The module is pinned at a commit of its `master` (`v0.1.1-0.20260612195517-5625512f24f6`): its last release (v0.1.0, 2024) has no `ipv6_prefix`, which counts an IPv6 /64 as one visitor.
  - The numbers rest on a measurement in a browser: one request per action to the limited routes (no prefetching, nothing while typing in the search box), three for an editor's sign-in.
  - The module logs each refused request with the visitor's whole address; that logger is excluded from Caddy's log, and a test fails without the exclusion.
  - The limits come before the password, so a wrong password counts too, and the right one does not get past a limit.
- **Lighthouse** (#84; §17.2, §17.3): `pnpm lighthouse:prod` and `pnpm lighthouse:local` (`tools/lighthouse`). The password comes on standard input only; Lighthouse keeps the extra header in its settings and nowhere else in a report (checked with an invented value), and the script removes it, searches each report before writing it and the written files once more. After the review: the browser can find no other host than the site's in such a run (Chrome sends the header with every request of a page); the reports' folder is the script's own, and one that holds anything else is refused, never emptied (`--out .` would have deleted the repository); the password may hold only characters that are spelled one way everywhere. The local results are in §17.3.
- **Seen on the way, not changed:**
  - Caddy's `401` for a missing password carries none of the site's headers (no HSTS): the Caddyfile's header block, which also removes headers, is applied only to answers that pass through it. The `429` answer imports the headers itself.
  - Lighthouse 13.5 brings axe-core 4.14, whose experimental rule for a label's text in its name (WCAG 2.5.3) marks the language button ("EN", named "Language: English (EN)") on desktop; axe-core 4.13, which the tests use, passes it, and the rule has no weight in Lighthouse's score.
  - On a page with the map, Lighthouse's best-practices score is 96 locally: no basemap on this computer (a `404` in the console), and MapLibre's files name source maps that are not served. On the deployed site the console shows "Worker failed to load": the map's worker fetches `maplibre-gl-shared.mjs` itself, and Chrome does not add the run's password header to a worker's own requests (`401`; the page's own request for the file got `200`). Not a fault of the site: a visitor who typed the password in the browser's prompt sends it from workers too, and after the launch the map needs none.
- **On the server** (2026-10-07, with the operator's go-ahead for these steps):
  - the server check: `deploy/monitor` and the new `backup/alert.sh` copied (their checksums compared with `main`), the dry run read, the two units installed and the timer switched on. The first run's line: `ok: disk / 39%, memory 76% available, certificate gliagora.com 82 days, certificate www.gliagora.com 82 days`; `alert.sh` then says "alerts off (no SMTP_HOST …)", as it will until the mailbox. From outside afterwards: `/health/` answers `200`, pages `401`;
  - one read of `/root/site-password`, to pipe it into the Lighthouse run, found no such file (the runbook keeps it there only until the operator has saved it), so that run did not start;
  - the reviewed server check (#83), the same day: `monitor/check.sh`, its unit and `backup/alert.sh` copied again (checksums compared with `main`), the unit installed, the empty `/var/lib/gliagora-monitor` of the first version removed; its state is now in `/run/gliagora-monitor`, and a run that ends well writes only its `ok:` line;
  - not done: the Caddy image with the new `Caddyfile` and `compose.prod.yml` (the package must be public first), and no setting was changed.
- **On the server** (2026-10-16, with the operator's go-ahead in the session):
  - the Lighthouse run against the deployed site, with a temporary second user for the password gate: a random password generated on the server into a root-only file (never shown), a copy of `admin-auth.caddy` kept, one line added to it in place and Caddy reloaded; the password piped into `pnpm lighthouse:prod --user lighthouse`; then the copy written back in place, Caddy reloaded, the user checked to be refused (`401`) and both files deleted. The gate holds its one user again. The 66 written reports were searched once more for the password and the header's value: none held them;
  - still not done: the Caddy image (its package was still private from outside after the operator had changed it), so the rate limits are not on the server yet.
- **Open points:**
  - the operator's review of D51 (the limits, the thresholds, the image and its package): **approved on 2026-10-16, as built** (D51);
  - the Caddy image's package made public in GitHub, then the server steps of the runbook's section 16;

**M7 notes (2026-10-16; finding positions automatically, D52).** Built and tested locally on Node 24 and PostgreSQL 18 with fake data and fake web pages (no request left the computer in a test), and in CI; nothing public. Pull requests #87 (the decisions), #88 (sources, redaction, tag suggester), #89 (monitors), #90 (the first sources, the link check, the dashboard), #91 (AI drafts) and #92 (the operations script's wait, and this wrap-up).

- **Sources** (§5.1, `src/collections/Sources.ts`): kinds `rss`, `api` (Greenhouse, Lever, SmartRecruiters), `page` (a list in plain HTML with a pattern of its postings' addresses) and `manual`; the terms (address, what they say about automated access, the day an editor read them; "forbids" keeps a source manual), robots.txt (checked by every run), the schedule, "Monitor on", "Every posting here is in neuroscience", the health fields, "Needs attention" (three failed runs in a row, robots.txt or a `tdm-reservation` refusing, terms changed) and, for manual sources, "Looked at today". `source-postings`: each posting a monitor saw, by source and canonical address, with its redacted title and outcome, forgotten 180 days after its list last named it.
- **The first sources** (migration `20261016_120000_seed_sources`): the 70 employers of `docs/sources/initial-sources.csv`, researched on 2026-10-16 from their official sites, with sub-units' own lists; 118 sources, 13 RSS, 44 list pages, 61 manual; the list with every source's kind and reason is `docs/sources/sources-2026-10-16.csv`. Three duplicates were left out (Helsinki's Finnish list, the same ads with other IDs; CNRS's list of all 444 offers, as six neuroscience units have feeds; IIT's whole list, as its neuroscience centre has its own). Kept manual: lists drawn by JavaScript (Workday, Oracle, SuccessFactors without a plain list, TalentLink, iCIMS portals, d.vinci, BITE), bot checks (Cloudflare, AWS WAF, Incapsula: Oxford's departments, OIST, Columbia, Johns Hopkins), ads written into one page or as PDFs only (SWC, Gatsby, ZNZ, HIH, i3S, UCT's Neuroscience Institute), robots.txt refusals (SmartRecruiters' API, Harvard's academic positions, the USAJOBS API, NEOGOV), Jobbnorge (a job board, so NTNU and its Kavli Institute), terms that forbid automated use (Institut Pasteur's research site, Weizmann, the University of Queensland), and, until the operator decides, terms that restrict storing the site's content (Radboud and the Donders Institute, ETH Zurich, the University of Tübingen). "Monitor on" started unticked, so the first run was by hand.
- **Monitors** (§6.3; `src/lib/monitors/`, `monitorSources` at 04:40 UTC in the `monitors` queue, which has a tick of its own): robots.txt (RFC 9309; unreadable means nothing is read; Crawl-delay up to 60 s), the list, the postings new since the last run, each new posting's page where robots.txt allows (JSON-LD JobPosting, else the title and the visible text without navigation), the neuroscience check (a neuroscience word in the title, or two in the text: stems of the field's words in the first sources' languages, Japanese, Chinese and Korean included, and the subfield synonyms that only neuroscience uses), the deadline (JSON-LD `validThrough` when it is a date, else the first date after a deadline word, in twelve languages; 3/11/2026 is in March only in the United States; a posting whose deadline has passed is skipped, since many lists keep closed posts), duplicates (an address a candidate or job has), then a candidate (New) with the redacted source text (at most 8,000 characters) and its facts, each saying where it came from. At most 40 posting pages per source and run; one request at a time; 3 s between two to one host; 20 s and 4 to 8 MB per request; the user agent `Gliagora-Monitor/1.0 (+https://gliagora.com/en/about/#reading-employers-pages)`; no cookies; session IDs removed from addresses (IBS). A source's failure is recorded on it and the run goes on. The terms page is read once a week; a change flags the source.
- **Redaction** (§6.6, `src/lib/redaction/redact.ts`), the one function used before anything is stored or exported: email addresses (also written with [at]), phone numbers (after a word that names them, international, or national in groups) and names after titles (Prof., Dr., …), before "Lab"/"Group" and after words such as "contact" or "supervised by", with lists of topic words and eponyms (Max Planck, Kavli) that are not names. A candidate's source text is redacted on every save. Names in Chinese, Japanese or Korean, and names the text does not mark, are not found.
- **Local tag suggester** (§6.3, `src/lib/tags/`): the taxonomy's labels and synonyms, capitalised acronyms in their case, the longest match per vocabulary; the position type from the title (in the text only by synonyms, since "a PhD in" names a requirement), career stages from the type when the text names none. Shown on candidates as "Suggested tags", with the words found.
- **Draft jobs** start from what a candidate offers: the AI draft (unless "Start the job from the AI draft" is unticked), the monitor's deadline, the suggested tags, with a note to check each.
- **Link check** (weekly, Sundays 05:30 UTC): every published job's original page; Gone, Broken, Redirects, and "the page says the position is filled or closed"; recorded in SQL (no version); "The link check's warning is wrong" holds until the finding changes. Nothing is closed automatically.
- **Dashboard**: a "Sources" box under "Waiting for an editor".
- **AI drafts** (§6.6, #91): the weekly run (`docs/weekly-drafts.md`, `tools/ai-drafts/weekly.sh`, the routes `/api/ops/ai-drafts/export/` and `/import/`) and the Claude API (`draftWithClaude`, 07:30 UTC, off until `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `AI_MONTHLY_CAP_USD` are set; the official SDK, structured output, model `claude-opus-5-5` unless `CLAUDE_MODEL` names another with a known price, the server-side refusal fallback, each request's cost recorded at once and the run stopped before a request that could pass the cap). Both send only `draftInput()` and store only through `applyDraft()`, which checks the draft's shape, terms and the hash of the input it was written from.
- **Operations routes** (`/api/ops/…`, `ops/call.mjs`): only inside the app's container, with a key derived from PAYLOAD_SECRET (`deploy/README.md` section 17).
- **Texts**: the About page has a new section, "Reading employers' pages", which the user agent links to (approved as written by the operator on 2026-10-17); the drafts of the sources policy, the privacy policy and the KVKK aydınlatma metni describe the monitors and the weekly drafts; the editor guide has sections 5 and 6.
- **The first run by hand** (2026-10-16, on the server, with the operator's go-ahead in the session): six sources of different kinds, through `/api/ops/monitors/run/` (#91 fixed the routes, which refused requests from inside the container: Next.js's server sets X-Forwarded-For to the connection's address; #92 lets `ops/call.mjs` wait longer than fetch's five minutes). Every source read without a failure, robots.txt allowing all six. **34 candidates**: Cambridge Clinical Neurosciences 2 (1 skipped as not neuroscience), KCL IoPPN 3, Karolinska's Varbi feed 12 (28 skipped; 40 of its 84 postings read, the cap, the rest at the next run), the Max Planck Society's job board 1 (29 of its 30 skipped: most of its posts are in other fields), the Netherlands Institute for Neuroscience 4, RIKEN CBS 12. No posting was skipped as closed or as a duplicate. Deadlines: 19 of the 34 have a day (Varbi's "Last application date 25.Oct.2026", Cambridge's "Closing Date: 1st November 2026", KCL's and the Max Planck Society's JobPosting `validThrough`, NIN's "apply before December 1, 2026"), 11 "until filled" (RIKEN), 4 none; 32 have suggested subfields and 29 a position type. Probable false positives: a few of Karolinska's (a research assistant in pharmacoepidemiology, one on blood data for PET), whose text names the employing department, Clinical Neuroscience, and RIKEN CBS lists posts from 2025 that may be filled ("open until filled"). Only counts, outcomes and the postings' redacted titles were read in the session, never the candidates' own text.
- **Seen on the way:** several sources that read as manual could be read with a small adapter: Oxford's CoreHR lists (the posting address can be built from the ID in the list), Janelia (the postings are JSON in an attribute of a custom element), and the Workday sites of HHMI, CSHL, McGill, UBC, DZNE, Helmholtz Munich and EMBL, whose robots.txt names a sitemap of posting pages. Feeds and APIs that exist but robots.txt refuses: SmartRecruiters' API (Harvard), Harvard's academic Atom feed, SuccessFactors' feeds under `/services/` (Helsinki, EPFL, Toronto, Duke-NUS) and the USAJOBS API. The University of Helsinki publishes each ad in English and Finnish with different IDs (only the English list is a source). The Hebrew University's hosts did not answer from the computer that did the research (whether the server reaches them is unknown). Redaction cannot find names written in Chinese, Japanese or Korean (CEBSIT's Chinese lists name groups by their heads).
- **Switched on** (2026-10-16, the operator's decision in the session): all 57 automatic sources, through `/api/ops/monitors/switch-on/`, at 20:57 UTC; the schedule's first run with them is on 2026-10-17 at 04:40 UTC, the six sources of the first run included. **Kept manual** (the operator's decision of 2026-10-16): the five sources whose terms restrict storing content (Radboud and Donders, ETH Zurich twice, the University of Tübingen).
- **Open points:** (1) the operator's look at the first run's candidates in the admin; (2) none for the five kept manual; (3) the About page's new section: approved as written by the operator on 2026-10-17; (4) the Claude API: a key, a monthly cap and the model, when the operator wants it (`deploy/README.md` section 17); (5) lawyer questions B17 and B22; (6) 61 manual sources, all on a weekly schedule: the operator may set some to monthly.

**M11 notes (2026-10-17; the newsletter builder and the subfield introductions, D53).** Built and tested locally on Node 24 and PostgreSQL 18 with fake data, a real listmonk 6.2 (the release binary) and Mailpit, and in CI; nothing public, no email sent. Pull requests #96 (the decisions), #97 (the subfield introductions), #98 (issues, the email, preview and test send) and #99 (listmonk, the push, the campaigns' check, the archive, these notes). #98 and #99 were each reviewed before they were pushed by an independent reviewer, whose findings were fixed in them (ten in #98: among them Payload's relationship check refusing saves once a selected job closed, `&lt;` in the plain text, the size cap not enforced and employer links re-serialised).

- **Subfield introductions** (D53, #97): a field "AI draft of the introduction" (`hubIntroDraft`, localized, editors only: field rules and the public queries' own `select`; a test keeps every other reader out), filled where empty by a migration with 19 English drafts (191–206 words; `docs/subfield-introductions.md`, which a test keeps equal to the data); "Hub introduction" stays the only text shown and counted (D40). The editor guide's section 7 and the sources policy's AI section say so.
- **Issues** (`newsletter-issues`, §5.1; #98): one a month, the month from the date (unique); the builder proposes 12 selected positions (the newest still open a week after the issue's date, regions taking turns, each with a subfield not chosen yet, leaving out the previous issue's) on creation and when "Propose … again" is ticked; the deadlines are the fixed deadlines from the issue's date to 45 days later, at most 40 (the rest a link to the jobs list); a selected position that will have closed by the issue's date is left out. Status, campaign, times and snapshot are the site's; the editor's fields lock while listmonk has the campaign scheduled, sending, paused or sent; an issue that reached listmonk cannot be deleted. Payload's own relationship check would have refused every save once a selected job had closed, so the field checks only the number. The launch issue (D41) is saved as a draft by a migration, with a subject, a preheader and a draft opening note.
- **The email** (§13): React Email (`@react-email/*` 0.x components and `render` 2.1.0, server only), 600px, one column, system fonts, the site's colours written out from the tokens (a test checks each) with dark mode for `prefers-color-scheme` and Outlook.com's markers, no image (nothing loads when it is opened), the footer of §13; the plain text from the same content; UTM tags on links to our pages only, employers' links with only tracking parameters removed (byte for byte otherwise), unsubscribe and legal links without tags. Over 100 KB of HTML (titles of 300 characters outside ASCII can get there), the last deadlines move into the jobs-list link until it fits. The sample from the fake data (`docs/glia-notes-sample/`): 34.8 KB of HTML, 10.9 KB of text.
- **listmonk's templates**: listmonk 6.2 reads a campaign's body and plain text as Go `html/template`, its subject and headers as `text/template`, after rewriting its shorthands (`…@TrackLink`). So every `{{` from the site is printed through `{{"{{"}}`, `@Track` is broken by a zero-width space, and every `<` of the plain text goes through listmonk's `Safe` (html/template would print `&lt;`); only then do the unsubscribe links get `{{ .Subscriber.ID }}` and `{{ .Subscriber.UUID }}`. **The unsubscribe headers**: listmonk 6.2 fills template values in a campaign's own headers (models/campaigns.go, `HeaderTpls`), so the campaign carries `List-Unsubscribe: <…/en/newsletter/unsubscribe/use/?id={{ .Subscriber.ID }}&s={{ .Subscriber.UUID }}>` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post: List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` itself, and listmonk's built-in header (`privacy.unsubscribe_header`, its own unreachable address) stays off; a database test runs a real campaign to two fake subscribers through Mailpit and checks that each gets their own ID and UUID in both headers and links, and the site's text exactly as written.
- **Preview and test send** (#98): the issue's sidebar in the admin (`src/admin/IssueActions.tsx`): the HTML and the plain text (`GET /api/newsletter-issues/:id/preview/`, a strict CSP), a test email through the app's mail server to the editor and `GLIA_NOTES_SEED_ADDRESSES` (`POST …/test/`, same origin only; `sendOne()` can carry an HTML part), with headers naming listmonk's preview UUID, so its unsubscribe links change nothing. A scheduled or sent issue previews its snapshot.
- **The push** (#99): `POST …/push/` with `draft` or `schedule`, only while `GLIA_NOTES_PUSH=on` (off on the server until the launch, D53), with the issues' own API user (`LISTMONK_CAMPAIGNS_TOKEN`) and the mailbox's limits, and scheduling also with `SMTP_HOST`. It builds the content, fits it under 100 KB, makes or updates the campaign (a scheduled one goes back to a draft first, so a failure leaves nothing due), records the campaign and the snapshot at once, then schedules it after the editor confirms. A campaign listmonk is sending, has paused or has sent is never changed; a cancelled one is replaced by a new one.
- **listmonk** (`deploy/listmonk/settings.sql`): the site's campaign template (`{{ template "content" . }}`, never the default); the user `gliagora-campaigns` with `campaigns:get`, `campaigns:manage`, `campaigns:send` and `templates:get`, and Glia Notes with `list:get` in its list role (no subscriber, no setting, never `subscribers:sql_query`; disabled while its token is not set; tests check what it may and may not do); one message a second from one worker, 25 errors pause a campaign, and the sliding window from the mailbox's limits. **Sending rate**: of each mailbox limit (`MAILBOX_LIMIT_HOUR`, `MAILBOX_LIMIT_DAY`), a tenth stays for the scheduled emails and anything sent by hand and twice the forms' cap (`EMAIL_CAP_*`: their links, and as many welcome emails again) for the forms; the rest is the issues' share: per hour each hour, or with a daily limit a window of `ceil(24 / floor(day share / rate))` hours, so the bursts of a day stay within its share. A limit that leaves no share stops `prepare.sh` with a message. `settings.sql` computes it, the app mirrors it for its messages and start line (`Glia Notes issues: push off (GLIA_NOTES_PUSH, until the launch)`), and a database test checks that the two agree.
- **The campaigns' check** (`syncGliaNotesIssues`, every 10 minutes at :04, :14, …): each issue in listmonk takes its campaign's status, and `sentAt` (when listmonk started sending) once sent. A campaign listmonk does not show is left alone and counted as failed (listmonk answers the issues' user the same for a campaign that is gone and one it may not see).
- **Archive** (`/en/newsletter/archive/`, `/en/newsletter/{yyyy-mm}/`): from the snapshot, without the email's footer or UTM tags; the proxy answers 404 for a month without a sent issue (`src/lib/detail-routes.ts`, with the signup's own pages reserved); indexed (the archive once it lists an issue), in the pages sitemap; private until the launch (D39). `pnpm fake:jobs` adds a fake sent issue for the tests.
- **On the server**: nothing by hand. The app's changes and the migrations (the drafts, the issues, the launch draft, the task) arrive with the deploys; the `deploy/` changes (`compose.prod.yml`, `listmonk/settings.sql`, `listmonk/prepare.sh`) wait for M2's steps (`deploy/README.md` section 18); until then pushing is off anyway.
- **After the review of #99**: one push at a time (a PostgreSQL lock, the issue read again under it), so two editors never make two campaigns; taking a scheduled issue back to a draft works also while pushing is off (an emergency stop); after a failed call the push reads listmonk's real state and says it; a campaign listmonk no longer shows (it answers the issues' user 403 for one that is gone) gets a clear refusal, and `deploy/README.md` section 18 has the reset; the push refuses an `EMAIL_CAP_*` that the app would only correct, since listmonk's settings refuse it; `prepare.sh` does not restart a listmonk that is sending (its sending window lives in memory); a cancelled issue's new schedule warns that some subscribers may get it twice.
- **Accepted risk**: listmonk 6.2 cannot narrow the issues' user further: `campaigns:manage` also allows a campaign's test send to subscribers named by address (so a leaked token could tell whether an address is subscribed, and mail a subscriber), and `campaigns:send` starts a draft at once. The token lives only in the server's `.env` and the app's container, and listmonk only on the server's loopback.
- **Points for review (D53)**: the launch issue as a seeded draft (with a draft opening note in `src/migrations/data/launch-issue.ts`); the proposal's rule; at most 40 deadlines; the sending-rate rule and its reserve (a tenth, and the forms' cap twice); no images in the email (no logo: it would load from our server when opened); the archive private until the launch and indexed then; a campaign deleted in listmonk's admin is not noticed by the site (campaigns are made and changed only through the site).

**Pre-launch checklist** (after S, before the M6 public launch; M6 works through it together with the §3.4 gate):

- Lighthouse with the §17.3 budgets against the deployed site (production, behind the password, while there is no staging, D42), Core Web Vitals "good" in lab tests: `pnpm lighthouse:prod`, run from the operator's computer, not in CI (§17.2; built 2026-10-07). The local build passes (§17.3), and so did the run against the deployed site on 2026-10-16 (§17.3, with a temporary second user for the password gate, removed afterwards). Run it again before the launch, once the server has real positions (its lists are empty until then, and the job page is not measured).
- Rate limits in Caddy for the auth routes, the forms and search (D42): built and tested on 2026-10-07 (§18.2, "Rate limits in Caddy"; D51). **Still to do: put them on the server** (`deploy/README.md`, section 16, with the operator's go-ahead), which needs the Caddy image's package made public in GitHub first. Besides them, as before: the site-wide password in front of `/admin` and `/api`, Payload's lockout and reset limit, the forms' own rate limits, and search's limits on concurrent institution searches.
- In the first month after the launch, once a week: how many requests got `429`, per zone and as counts only, never addresses (Caddy's access log). Raise a limit if real people meet it: people behind one IPv4 address (a campus, a conference) share its counts (D51, approved 2026-10-16).
- The server check's emails (disk, memory, certificate; §18.2, "Monitoring"): built and put on the server on 2026-10-07 (D51; `deploy/README.md`, section 15). Its emails start with the backup emails, at M2; until then `journalctl -u gliagora-monitor` has its lines.
- Screen-reader walkthrough of every template on the deployed site: Orca (Linux), VoiceOver (iOS or macOS) and TalkBack (Android), plus keyboard-only, zoom and forced-colours checks (§17.2).
- Real-device check on a mid-range Android phone over throttled 4G (§17.2).
- Legal texts: the one-time lawyer review of the questions in `docs/legal-open-questions.md` (group b) happens between S and M2 (D32); the operator then approves each text (D28). The contact pages, privacy policy, KVKK aydınlatma metni, açık rıza metni and, with its form, the data-requests page (M2 row, D49) go live at M2; all other required legal pages are live before M6 (§3.4).
- axe: 0 serious or critical issues; SPF, DKIM and DMARC pass; backup restore drill passed (§3.4).
- Content: enter the first 150 positions (open; postponed by the operator on 2026-10-01, D47). The §3.4 gate needs at least 150 active verified positions across at least 25 countries; the scheduled jobs, the review queue and the editor guide in the admin (`/admin/help/`) are ready for it.
- The map in public (M5): drop the site-wide password from Caddy's `/basemap/` handle and give `/api/map/institutions/` its own handle without it (`deploy/Caddyfile`), both behind the password until then (D39); send the map data's answers as `public` instead of `private`; and add a job-page credit for the time zones if lawyer question B20 asks for one.
- Member sign-in (M4b, D49; `deploy/README.md` step 9; each step on the server with the operator's go-ahead): register the production Google and ORCID clients (the steps open that runbook section; redirect addresses `https://gliagora.com/en/sign-in/google/callback/` and `https://gliagora.com/en/sign-in/orcid/callback/`); copy the deploy files (`Caddyfile`, `compose.prod.yml`, `backup/backup.sh`, `backup/restore.sh`) **before** setting any sign-in key or `SMTP_HOST`; then set the keys. The first ORCID sign-in (the operator's, behind the password) is a gate: it succeeds only if ORCID echoes the nonce and accepts the token request without PKCE; otherwise the log names `OAUTH_JWT_CLAIM_COMPARISON_FAILED` or the token error, and ORCID cannot be used until that changes. Publish the Google consent screen ("In production") at M6; until then it stays in "Testing", with the operator's accounts as test users. At the launch, open `/en/sign-in/`, `/en/sign-out/` and `/en/account/` in Caddy, and the data-request sub-paths too, unless M2 opened them with the approved page.
- Remove, once, the Docker volumes that earlier versions of `restore.sh test` left on the server (one full copy of the database per run since S; `deploy/README.md` step 7), with the operator's go-ahead; the new `restore.sh` removes its own, so copy it first.
- Private until the launch (D39): jobs and the other sections (search, institution pages, subfield hubs, the suggest and report forms, accounts) go public only at the M6 launch, once the §3.4 gate is met (at least 150 active verified positions across at least 25 countries, and the rest of the gate). At the launch, the reduced M2 header and footer give way to the full ones, and the links that point to `hello@gliagora.com` in place of the suggest and report forms point to the forms again (D41).

Phase 1 total: about 23–32 weeks (including S). If M0 starts in early October 2026, the public launch (M6) lands around the turn of the year (late December to early January), still inside the PhD application season.

**Conventions to be written into `CLAUDE.md` at M0 (preview)**

- Conversation in Turkish; code, UI text and documentation in English.
- TypeScript strict; ESLint + Prettier; no `any`; small, typed modules.
- Server Components by default; `"use client"` only for interactive parts.
- UI strings only through next-intl messages; colours, spacing and type only through design tokens.
- Semantic HTML first; every interactive element keyboard-reachable; axe tests for every new template.
- Never add third-party scripts, fonts, embeds or services without a `docs/vendors.md` entry and a KVKK check; never log or paste personal data.
- Content rules: facts + own summary + link; no copied text; employer-origin listings only.
- Payload migrations only; backup before migrating; every milestone deploys to staging first (production behind the site-wide password while there is no staging, D42).
- Definition of done: tests green, budgets met, a11y checks pass, docs updated, deployed to staging (see above) and reviewed on a phone.

---

## 21. Risks and mitigations

| # | Risk | Likelihood / impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A new tool quietly sends personal data abroad (KVKK) | Medium / High | Vendor register with a KVKK check; CSP blocks third-party origins; review at each milestone |
| 2 | Turkish SMTP relay has weaker deliverability than global providers | Medium / Medium | Full authentication and alignment; test sends; Postmaster Tools; a second Turkish relay evaluated as fallback |
| 3 | Latency for distant visitors from a server in Türkiye | Medium / Medium | Small server-rendered pages, caching, HTTP/3; real-user monitoring; Option H decision gate (§11.4) |
| 4 | Single-server failure | Low / High | Nightly off-site backups in Türkiye; tested restore; rebuild script; uptime alerts |
| 5 | Database-right or copyright complaint from a board or publisher | Low / Medium | No scraping; employer links; own words; takedown process; source register with evidence |
| 6 | İŞKUR treats aggregation as intermediation | Low / High | Strict publisher guardrails; employer-origin only; clear statements; pause the jobs window if contacted |
| 7 | Stale, expired or broken listings erode trust | High / Medium | Auto-expiry, weekly link checks, "last verified" dates, report-a-problem links |
| 8 | Curation burnout for a solo operator | High / High | Capped weekly volume; batch days; local tag suggestions and optional AI drafts; recruit a volunteer editor before Phase 3 |
| 9 | Bus factor (only one person knows the system) | Medium / High | `CLAUDE.md`, runbooks, infrastructure in the repo, password manager with emergency access |
| 10 | Framework churn (Next.js, Payload under new ownership) | Medium / Medium | Pinned versions; quarterly upgrades via staging; MIT licence allows forking |
| 11 | 3D hero or map hurts performance or accessibility | Medium / Medium | Progressive enhancement, eligibility checks, budgets enforced in CI, static fallbacks |
| 12 | Legal texts written without a lawyer contain gaps | Medium / Medium | Conservative design; flagged items (§15.2); review at every phase gate |
| 13 | Spam through the suggestion form | High / Low | Proof-of-work, honeypot, rate limits; nothing auto-publishes |
| 14 | Medical misinformation in Learn | Low / High | Evidence labels, references, disclaimers, reviewer, corrections log |
| 15 | Name or trademark conflict with "Gliagora" | Low / Medium | Search TÜRKPATENT, EUIPO and WIPO before investing in brand assets `[DECISION]` |
| 16 | Image licence mistakes | Medium / Low | Required licence and attribution fields in the media library; no logos |
| 17 | AI draft introduces errors | Medium / Medium | Human approval required; AI sees only source text; facts fields always checked against the original |
| 18 | EU representative and DSA costs arrive before revenue | Medium / Low | Budgeted at gates; Phase 3 scope kept minimal |

---

## 22. Assumptions and open questions

### 22.1 Assumptions (used throughout this plan)

1. The operator is a natural person resident in Türkiye, with no company and no revenue at launch.
2. A monthly budget of roughly $20–70 is acceptable at launch (plus the EU representative if chosen).
3. English only at launch, with Turkish legal texts from day one.
4. No user-generated content is published in Phase 1; suggestions become editorial content after review.
5. Resolved: the final 6b logo SVG files are in `public/brand/` (M0); M13 builds the hero on them.
6. The operator reviews each milestone on the deployed site from a phone (production behind the site-wide password while there is no staging, D42) and approves production releases.
7. Minimum age for the newsletter is 16.
8. Turkish-registered hosting, mail and relay providers meeting the requirements in §11.4 are available at the listed price ranges `[U]`.

Scan of 26 Sep 2026: ≈200 unique open neuroscience positions on five boards, ≈120–140 of them closing within 30 days, in ≥23 countries (SfN NeuroJobs and EURAXESS not counted) | — | — |

a static "Account" link in the header (an icon from 1280px) with an account sub-navigation on the account pages; Save is a POST route with a small lazily loaded enhancement; D35 by a `prefs=off` list parameter, the member's subfields applied only to the bare jobs list; every change of sign-in methods ends the member's other sessions; email links are bound to the browser that asked (a warning elsewhere); public-form Server Actions refuse forwarded calls; one email budget per address shared by every form that sends an email, plus a site-wide cap; data requests are acted on only after the person confirms them and are recorded minimally at once (kind, date, keyed address hash, 60 days); a confirmed deletion also deletes a member account with that address and clears that address on problem reports (personal-rights reports keep it); a deletion log is re-applied after a restore; no final email after an inactivity deletion; members without an email address are deleted 24 months and 30 days after their last activity, without warning; cookies `__Host-gliagora-session` and `__Host-gliagora-signin`; the test issuer and ORCID's sandbox are refused in production; the CI e2e app runs on the host network; the approved data-requests page goes public at M2 with its form. **Approved by the operator on 2026-10-02 as built**, with every point the M4b notes list under "Open points", and one addition: a member without an email address (ORCID only, or Google with an address Google does not manage) is told on the account page that we cannot warn them before their account is deleted after 24 months without activity, and that adding an address lets us warn them (and lets them join Glia Notes); the privacy drafts say the same (built with D50) | §2.2–2.4, §3.2, §5.1, §7.4–7.5, §9.12, §11.3, §11.5, §13, §15.4, §17.2–17.3, §18, §20, §22.2, CLAUDE.md |

nothing depends on it (a failing listmonk never blocks an app deploy); the site runs the double opt-in itself, so listmonk holds only confirmed subscribers and a pending signup is an email link in the app's database (7 days, left out of backups); every page and email a subscriber sees is the app's (listmonk will only send the issues); unsubscribe links carry listmonk's subscriber ID and UUID, and the one-click route is the one POST route without an origin check; the consent log holds listmonk's subscriber UUID and ID and never an address, `requested` is written at confirmation with the time of the form, and the records are kept 3 years after the subscription ended, carried over a restore and unlinked (not anonymised) on deletion; unsubscribes and erasures go into the deletion log, which deletes the subscriber again after a restore; members' choices are synced by a task (with one attempt at once), an account takes over a subscription its verified address made through the form, and after a restore a member is subscribed again only when the records show no withdrawal; an inactivity deletion leaves Glia Notes alone (§22.2, question 11); the M2 state is one setting read by Caddy and the app, with static twins of the public pages, one list of public pages, exact addresses in Caddy and marks between Caddy and the app; the newsletter goes public only with the approved açık rıza metni; the email cap never rises above a value set; container logs go to the journal (14 days, no copy for syslog); backup emails on every failure and on the Sunday backup's and the restore test's success. **Approved by the operator on 2026-10-06 as built**, with every point the M2 notes list under "Points for review" (confirmed by the operator in the Claude Code session of 2026-10-16) | §2.2, §5.1, §9.10, §11.2–11.6, §13, §16.1, §17.3, §18, §20, `deploy/README.md`, CLAUDE.md |
| D51 | Decided 2026-10-06 (the operator's task file): the technical items of the pre-launch checklist that need neither the mailbox nor the lawyer are built now: Caddy's rate limits (D42 (3)), Lighthouse against the deployed site, the server check's emails, and drafts of the accessibility statement and of the sources and editorial policy. Not in it: Umami (its script against the jobs list's budget, the operator decides first), the checks by people (a real device, screen readers), the external uptime monitor (a new vendor), the restore drill, and M7. **Built 2026-10-07 (#80–#84); the design, for the operator's review:** the server runs a Caddy image of its own, the official one with the program built in CI from fixed sources plus `github.com/mholt/caddy-ratelimit` at one commit of its `master`, pushed to GHCR under a tag that holds a hash of its inputs and pinned by digest; the package must be public, since the server keeps no registry login (D27); four limits per visitor address (IPv6 by its /64), before the password: editors' sign-in and password routes 20 a minute, member sign-in 120, the public forms 30, search and filtered lists 120, each counted over 15 or 30 seconds so that the limiter's record per address stays small; `429` with `Retry-After` and one line of text, at the cost of the reason for a `502` in Caddy's own log; the module's own log line and its metrics are off; the one-click unsubscribe is not limited; people behind one IPv4 address share its counts; the `caddy` container has a memory limit (768 MB) and HTTP/3 early data is off; Lighthouse runs from the operator's computer, never in CI, as a project of its own under `tools/`, with the password on standard input only and reports that are cleaned of it and searched for it; a local run slows the browser down for real, since Lighthouse's estimate from a local load is the time until the whole page has loaded; the server check runs every 15 minutes, reports a problem once a second run sees it and then once a day (also one that comes and goes), counts a report only once its email has gone, keeps what it has seen in memory, and sends no email when a problem is over; a certificate is reported at a sixth of its lifetime, whatever its length; the accessibility statement names WCAG 2.1 AA and says which WCAG 2.2 checks run; the sources policy describes later sections in a few lines. **Approved by the operator on 2026-10-16, as built, with every point above**, and two additions to the pre-launch checklist (§20): in the first month after the launch, a weekly look at how many requests got `429`, per zone and as counts only (never addresses); and a limit is raised if real people meet it, since people behind one IPv4 address (a campus, a conference) share its counts | §11.3, §11.5, §17.2, §17.3, §18.2, §20, `deploy/README.md`, `docs/legal-open-questions.md`, CLAUDE.md |
